Table~\ref{tab:ce-ceilings} collects the values. At $\rho = 0.8$ the
compound-symmetry ceiling without carryover is 0.256 and the separable
AR(1) ceiling is 0.440 (Hybrid path A). At $\rho = 0.7$ the separable
AR(1) ceiling is 0.474 (OL+BDC path A), and 0.480 in the Hybrid design.
At $\rho = 0.8$, $c_{bm} = 0.3$ is feasible in every design under the
separable form, and $c_{bm} = 0.6$ exceeds every graded-coupling
ceiling in Hybrid and OL+BDC at every half-life examined (at most 0.544
and 0.578 at $t_{1/2} = 1$). At $\rho = 0.7$, $c_{bm} = 0.45$ is
feasible in every design under the separable form. (Source: \code{docs/34}, review
note; \code{docs/35}, Section 6; \code{docs/36}, Section 6.5; Paper 01,
``Room for larger effect sizes''.) A higher ceiling is not more
power: at the same $c_{bm}$, AR(1) data carry less interaction
information than compound-symmetric data, and \code{docs/35} and
Paper~01 report closed-form paired-difference power in Hybrid at
$c_{bm} = 0.25$ of 0.721 under compound symmetry against 0.335 under
the separable form. \note{The power pair is from \code{docs/37},
Section 5.8, via \code{docs/35} and Paper 01, neither of which states
the $\rho$ at which it holds. It is at $\rho = 0.8$, the published
value, with $c_1 = 0.2$ and no measurement error: in
\code{analysis/data/quick-sim/carryover-closed-form/design-power.csv}
the Hybrid rows at $t_{1/2} = 0$ give power 0.7209 (compound symmetry)
and 0.3347 (separable arm, whose ceiling 0.4400 is the $\rho = 0.8$
value of Table~\ref{tab:ce-ceilings}) (inspected), and the pair is
recomputed in Table~\ref{tab:inve-power-struct} (verified,
\code{check-closed-form-extensions.R}).}

\subsection{Ceilings by serial correlation and by cross-component form}
\label{sec:invd-ce-rho}

Table~\ref{tab:ce-ceilings} was extended in this revision to
$\rho = 0.6$ and $0.5$ and to the package's non-separable AR(1) form
(configuration F of \code{docs/36}, the \code{covar} construct: AR(1)
within components, cross-component correlation $c_1$ at the same visit
and $c_\times\rho^{|w_t - w_s|}$ otherwise,
Equation~\eqref{eq:ce-covar-pd}). Each entry is
Equation~\eqref{eq:ce-ceiling} with graded coupling minimized over the
design's paths. (Source: \code{docs/44}, Step 6; recomputed
independently, and all 144 cells of \code{ceilings-graded-by-rho.csv},
which also cover $t_{1/2} = 0.5$, 1 and 2 weeks, agree to
$6 \times 10^{-16}$, \code{check-stress-test-construct.R}, Section 1;
the Hybrid F entry at $\rho = 0.5$, 0.48855 unrounded, is given as
0.489, as in Table~\ref{tab:inve-crossed}'s source \code{docs/34},
Section 4.4, and checked in \code{check-reconciliations.R}.) Four statements
follow (verified, \code{check-stress-test-construct.R}, Section 1). (a) For every
structure and $\rho$ in the table, the design minimum over
$t_{1/2} \in \{0, 0.5, 1, 2\}$ weeks is attained at $t_{1/2} = 0$, so
the last column of Table~\ref{tab:ce-ceilings} is also the minimum over
those half-lives. Carryover never lowers a ceiling below its
no-carryover value except in CO at $\rho = 0.5$ and $t_{1/2} = 2$
weeks, by 0.0029 (F) and 0.0036 (C). (b) Separability raises the
ceiling most where serial correlation is strongest (0.291 to 0.440 at
$\rho = 0.8$) and hardly at all at $\rho = 0.5$ (0.448 either way);
under F at $\rho = 0.8$ the response block is nearly singular in
Hybrid and OL+BDC (Table~\ref{tab:inve-eig}). (c) Configuration C at
$\rho = 0.7$ admits $c_{bm} = 0.45$ in every design and half-life with
a margin of 0.024. (d) Compound symmetry has the lowest ceiling at
$\rho \ge 0.6$ and the highest at $\rho = 0.5$ (0.458 against 0.448);
the order of the structures depends on $\rho$.

\textbf{A constant pattern.} For an open-label path coupled at every
visit ($g = \mathbf 1$), on eight visits 2.5 weeks apart, at
$\rho = 0.8$ the ceiling is $(n a_n)^{-1/2} = 0.893$ under compound
symmetry, 0.576 under F and 0.567 under C (the OL rows of
Table~\ref{tab:inve-config}), and 0.535 under AR(1) with the published
constant cross-component block (verified, \code{check-open-label-ceiling.R}). \note{\code{docs/44}, Step 4, attributes the
fall from 0.893 to 0.576 to AR(1) ``applied with the published
cross-component form''. The value 0.576, like the Hybrid value 0.291,
is that of \code{docs/36}'s configuration B, whose cross-component
block is the decaying form $c_\times\rho^{|w_t - w_s|}$
(\code{docs/36}, Appendix A.6). With the published constant block the
values are 0.535 (open label) and 0.363 (Hybrid, $t_{1/2} = 0$). The
schedule of the open-label design was not stated in the sources; the
equally spaced 2.5-week schedule reproduces all three \code{docs/36}
values, which is the evidence for it.}

\subsection{Ceilings at the package value $\rho = 0.7$: the crossed
structures}
\label{sec:inve-ce-rho7}

\code{docs/34} crosses the two blocks that ``AR(1)'' touches and the
coupling rule: within-factor block compound symmetry or AR(1) in
calendar weeks; cross-factor entries at different visits constant
($c_\times$) or decaying ($c_\times\rho^{|w_t-w_s|}$); graded or step
coupling. At the paper~01 production parameters ($\rho = 0.7$,
$c_1 = 0.2$, $c_\times = 0.1$) the design-level ceilings are in
Table~\ref{tab:inve-crossed}; AR(1) with decaying cross-factor entries
and graded coupling is the package's \code{covar}, and compound symmetry
with constant entries and step coupling is the published specification
(Source: \code{docs/34}, Section 4.1; verified, every entry).

\begin{table}[h]
\centering\small
\caption{Design-level ceilings at $\rho = 0.7$, reported as
$t_{1/2} = 0$ / $0.5$ / $1$ weeks (Source: \code{docs/34}, Section 4.1;
verified).}
\label{tab:inve-crossed}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lllccc@{}}
\toprule
Within & Cross, $t\ne s$ & Coupling & CO & Hybrid & OL+BDC \\
\midrule
AR(1) & decaying & graded (\code{covar}) & .619 / .619 / .619 & .456 / .506 / .544 & .452 / .500 / .533 \\
AR(1) & decaying & step & .619 / .494 / .494 & .456 / .519 / .519 & .452 / .519 / .519 \\
AR(1) & constant & graded & .591 / .591 / .591 & .452 / .490 / .514 & .438 / .472 / .491 \\
AR(1) & constant & step & .591 / .430 / .430 & .452 / .462 / .462 & .438 / .463 / .463 \\
CS & constant & graded & .346 / .346 / .346 & .346 / .370 / .411 & .352 / .386 / .456 \\
CS & constant & step (published) & .346 / .346 / .346 & .346 / .841 / .841 & .352 / .841 / .841 \\
CS & decaying & graded & .164 / .164 / .164 & .247 / .265 / .299 & .151 / .170 / .214 \\
CS & decaying & step & .164 / .164 / .164 & .247 / .845 / .845 & .151 / .832 / .832 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

Under \code{covar} the binding path is path A (path 1) in Hybrid and
OL+BDC without carryover, switching to path B in Hybrid at
$t_{1/2} = 1$; in CO it is path A without carryover and the
placebo-first path B at positive half-lives (\code{docs/34}, Section
4.1; quoted). The reference value $c_{bm} = 0.45$ of paper~01 lies
within $0.002$ to $0.006$ of the \code{covar} ceiling in the
no-carryover Hybrid and OL+BDC cells. Four readings follow (derived from
the table).
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*), itemsep=2pt]
\item With the cross-factor block and graded coupling held fixed and
  $t_{1/2} = 0$, replacing compound symmetry by AR(1) raises the ceiling
  at $\rho = 0.7$ in every design: by $0.09$ to $0.25$ with constant
  entries and by $0.21$ to $0.45$ with decaying ones.
\item Compound symmetry with decaying cross-factor entries gives the
  lowest ceilings of all ($0.151$ in OL+BDC). It asserts that a
  factor's correlation with \emph{another} factor decays with lag while
  its correlation with itself does not, and its response block is then
  nearly singular (Table~\ref{tab:inve-eig}). Alongside AR(1) the same
  cross-factor decay costs nothing: it raises the ceiling by $0.004$ to
  $0.042$ across the graded cells of the table (CO: $0.591$ to
  $0.619$). \note{\code{docs/32}, Section 5.2, summarizes this as
  ``roughly $+0.02$ to $+0.03$''; the table gives $+0.004$ (Hybrid) to
  $+0.028$ (CO) without carryover and up to $+0.042$ (OL+BDC) at
  $t_{1/2} = 1$.}
\item The step-rule reversal (low ceiling without carryover, high with
  any) occurs in Hybrid and OL+BDC, whose paths all begin on drug, and
  not in CO, whose placebo-first path keeps pre-exposure zeros at every
  half-life. With an AR(1) within-factor block the step ceiling at
  positive half-lives is $0.519$, not $0.841$: the large jump requires
  both the step and compound symmetry.
\item Under \code{covar} the ceiling rises with the half-life in Hybrid
  and OL+BDC and is flat in CO, so a grid feasible at positive
  half-lives may be infeasible at $t_{1/2} = 0$; the no-carryover cell
  binds.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{table}[h]
\centering\small
\caption{Smallest eigenvalue of the response block $M$, minimum over
paths (Source: \code{docs/34}, Section 4.2, at $\rho = 0.7$;
\code{docs/36}, Section 6.5, at $\rho = 0.8$, where OL has the CO
schedule and the CO value; verified).}
\label{tab:inve-eig}
\begin{tabular}{@{}llccc@{}}
\toprule
$\rho$ & Response block & CO & Hybrid & OL+BDC \\
\midrule
0.7 & AR(1), decaying (\code{covar}) & 0.288 & 0.074 & 0.074 \\
0.7 & AR(1), constant & 0.331 & 0.093 & 0.093 \\
0.7 & CS, constant (published) & 0.200 & 0.200 & 0.200 \\
0.7 & CS, decaying & 0.034 & 0.039 & 0.015 \\
0.8 & CS, constant (published) & 0.100 & 0.100 & 0.100 \\
0.8 & AR(1), decaying (\code{covar}) & 0.153 & 0.010 & 0.010 \\
0.8 & Separable AR(1) & 0.225 & 0.097 & 0.098 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

The compound-symmetry value is $(1-\rho_{cs})-(c_1-c_\times)$ exactly
(Section~\ref{sec:ce-cs}). The AR(1) values depend on the design
because the one-week gaps of Hybrid and OL+BDC make adjacent visits
strongly correlated. On every grid of \code{docs/34} and \code{docs/36}
the response block itself is valid, so every failure is a ceiling
failure. \note{At $\rho_{cs} = 0.8$ compound symmetry with decaying
cross-factor entries is not positive definite in any design (smallest
eigenvalues $-0.082$, $-0.056$, $-0.095$; computed here, not in either
source).}

\textbf{Dependence on $\rho$.} Table~\ref{tab:ce-ceilings} gives the
no-carryover ceilings of the compound-symmetry, \code{covar} and
separable structures at $\rho = 0.5$ to 0.8; Table~\ref{tab:inve-rho}
adds the end points $\rho = 0.3$ and 0.9. Under compound symmetry the
ceiling falls steadily with $\rho_{cs}$ and $M$ reaches singularity at
$\rho_{cs} = 0.9$, where $(1-\rho_{cs})-(c_1-c_\times) = 0$. Under \code{covar}
the ceiling rises with $\rho$ through $0.8$ in CO, and on the grid
$\rho \in \{0.3, 0.5, 0.6, 0.7, 0.8\}$ peaks at $\rho = 0.5$ in Hybrid
and at $\rho = 0.6$ in OL+BDC; at $\rho = 0.8$ it has fallen steeply in
both, and at $0.9$ their response block fails. The statement ``AR(1)
widens the feasible range of $c_{bm}$'' therefore holds at
$\rho = 0.7$ and fails at $\rho = 0.3$, where compound symmetry admits
the higher ceiling in all three designs (Source: \code{docs/34},
Section 4.4, and \code{docs/44}, Step 6; verified). \note{\code{docs/34}
evaluates $\rho = 0.3$, 0.5, 0.7, 0.8 and 0.9 only; on that grid the
OL+BDC ceiling rises through 0.7, and the peak at 0.6 appears with the
$\rho = 0.6$ row of \code{docs/44}.}

verified). ``not PD'': $M$ itself is not positive definite.}
\label{tab:inve-rho}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lcc@{}}

\textbf{An evenly spaced schedule.} On eight visits two weeks apart, on
drug at the first four, at $\rho = 0.7$ the published specification has
ceiling $0.346$ without carryover and $0.841$ at any positive
half-life, against $0.670$ for \code{covar} with graded coupling at
$t_{1/2} = 1$ (at $\rho = 0.8$ the published values are $0.256$ and
$0.893$). Holding the graded coupling at $t_{1/2} = 1$ fixed, the
ceiling is $0.370$ under compound symmetry with constant cross-factor
entries, $0.646$ when only the within-factor block is made AR(1), and
$0.670$ when the cross-factor entries also decay (Source:
\code{docs/32}, Sections 3.3 and 4.3; verified). The like-for-like
comparison attributes almost all of the gain to the within-factor form;
the \code{orig}-against-\code{covar} comparison mixes two changes and
reverses direction. \note{\code{docs/32}, Section 5.2, also quotes a
fall from $0.34$ to $0.16$ when cross-factor decay is applied without
AR(1), from \code{docs/54} under the 2024
coupling that leaves the first visit uncoupled; that figure was not
recomputed. With every visit coupled the worst cell is $0.151$
(Table~\ref{tab:inve-crossed}).}

\textbf{The cross-factor parameters.} Under compound symmetry they
enter almost entirely through $c_1 - c_\times$, as
Equation~\eqref{eq:ce-cs-decomp} implies; under AR(1) the difference
dominates, but the levels matter and a large difference invalidates $M$
(Table~\ref{tab:inve-cross}).

\label{sec:inve-ce-rho8}

\code{docs/36} evaluates three proposed adjustments to the published
construct (A: compound symmetry, constant cross-factor entries, step
coupling): AR(1) within factors (B, step coupling, \code{covar} cross
block), AR(1) with graded coupling (F, \code{covar} cross block; C,
separable), and mean moderation (D on compound symmetry, E on the
separable block), which has no ceiling. The OL design (eight visits
$2.5$ weeks apart, all on drug) is included (Source: \code{docs/36},
Section 6.5; verified, every entry; the OL schedule was identified by
reproducing its ceilings).

\noindent \note{The OL rows at $t_{1/2} = 2$ repeat the constant value,
since an all-on-drug path has no off-drug visit; the separable step
rows at $t_{1/2} = 0.1$ to 2 repeat the \code{docs/37} ``$t_{1/2} > 0$''
value, which holds wherever the off-drug mean has not underflowed
(Section~\ref{sec:inve-cg-moments}).}

Three consequences follow (derived from the table and
Equation~\eqref{eq:ce-ceiling}). First, AR(1) alone does not repair
problem~1: it raises the ceiling where the coupling has an on/off
contrast and \emph{lowers} it where the pattern is constant, as in OL
(from $0.893$ to $0.576$) and in every Hybrid and OL+BDC carryover cell.
The quantity $c_{bm}^2\tilde g^\top M^{-1}\tilde g$ is the squared
multiple correlation of the biomarker on the whole trajectory; under
compound symmetry the visits share a large person-level component that
the biomarker can correlate with once, whereas under AR(1) little is
shared, so the same correlation at every visit demands more of the
biomarker. Second, graded coupling alone keeps the low ceiling
($0.256$ to $0.265$ for $t_{1/2} \le 0.2$), so applied alone it would
make $c_{bm} = 0.3$ and $0.6$ infeasible in every Hybrid and OL+BDC
cell. Third, only the separable combination makes $c_{bm} = 0.3$
feasible everywhere; \code{covar} is marginal in Hybrid ($0.291$), and
$c_{bm} = 0.6$ exceeds every covariance-moderation ceiling in Hybrid and
OL+BDC at $\rho = 0.8$.

\textbf{Compound symmetry against the separable form under graded
coupling.} Under the published step rule and any $t_{1/2} > 0$ the
compound-symmetry ceiling exceeds the separable AR(1) one, because a
constant coupling pattern is cheap under compound symmetry
(Section~\ref{sec:ce-cs}, item 3). Under graded coupling the ordering
does not reverse: at $\rho = 0.8$ the separable ceiling is the higher
of the two in every design at every half-life examined (rows C and
``graded alone'' of Table~\ref{tab:inve-config}), and the
compound-symmetry ceiling is at most 0.469 (OL+BDC, $t_{1/2} = 2$)
(Source: \code{docs/33}, Section~3.4, note of 2026-10-07; \code{docs/44},
Step 6; verified, \code{check-landmarks-hybrid-ceilings.R}, block 10).
\note{The pre-restatement Paper~01 (Appendix~B.7) reported that for
any $t_{1/2} > 0$ the published compound-symmetry implementation admits
the higher ceiling; \code{docs/33}, Section 3.4, records that this is a
property of the step rule, under which the interaction is zero at every
positive half-life, so the higher ceiling carries no signal.}

\textbf{The ceiling predicts every repair.} A path's matrix fails the
published check (\code{corpcor::}\allowbreak\code{is.positive.definite()}) if and only if
$c_{bm} \ge c_{bm}^{\ast}$ for that path, since the response block is
valid in every cell (Table~\ref{tab:inve-eig}). Counting one matrix per
design path, $c_{bm}$ and $t_{1/2}$ gives Table~\ref{tab:inve-repair}
(Source: \code{docs/36}, Section 6.5, where the count was checked
against the \code{58b32a9} code's own repairs and against all 504
matrices; verified here from the ceilings).

\begin{table}[h]
\centering\small
\caption{Matrices requiring repair, by configuration. Published grid:
OL, OL+BDC, CO, Hybrid (nine paths), $c_{bm} \in \{0.3, 0.6\}$,
$t_{1/2} \in \{0, 0.1, 0.2\}$. Common grid: the same paths,
$c_{bm} \in \{0.25, 0.3\}$, $t_{1/2} \in \{0, 0.1, 0.2, 0.5, 1\}$
(Source: \code{docs/36}, Section 6.5; verified).}
\label{tab:inve-repair}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lcc@{}}
\toprule
Configuration & Published grid & Common grid \\
\midrule
A published & 20/54 (37\%) & 12/90 (13\%) \\
B AR(1) & 26/54 (48\%) & 3/90 (3\%) \\
F AR(1) + graded, \code{covar} & 26/54 (48\%) & 5/90 (6\%) \\

\textbf{The \code{covar} boundary at the published values.} With
$(c_1, c_\times) = (0.2, 0.1)$ the threshold of
Equation~\eqref{eq:ce-covar-pd} is
$(c_1-c_\times)/(1-c_\times) = 1/9 = 0.111$. For equally spaced visits
the smallest eigenvalue of $A$ stays above $(1-\rho^d)/(1+\rho^d)$
(Section~\ref{sec:inve-ce-covar}), so at $\rho = 0.8$ validity for any
number of visits is guaranteed only for gaps $d \ge 1$ week, exactly
the closest spacing of Hybrid and OL+BDC (derived; \code{docs/36},
Section 6.4). This is why the \code{covar} block in those designs has
smallest eigenvalue $0.010$ (Table~\ref{tab:inve-eig}).

\subsection{Schedule design under AR(1)}
\label{sec:inve-ce-schedule}

Under compound symmetry and binary coupling the ceiling depends only
on $n$, $n_{\mathrm{on}}$ and the correlation parameters
(Equation~\eqref{eq:ce-cs-closed}); \code{docs/34} reports that it is
$0.346$ in all twenty 8-visit, 4-on schedules it examined, while the
AR(1) ceiling ranges from $0.33$ to $0.74$ across the same schedules
(quoted). Under AR(1) every schedule feature moves the ceiling
(Table~\ref{tab:inve-schedule}; \code{covar}, $\rho = 0.7$, no
carryover).

\begin{table}[h]
\centering\small
\caption{Effect of schedule features on the AR(1) (\code{covar})
ceiling, $\rho = 0.7$, $c_1 = 0.2$, $c_\times = 0.1$, no carryover
(Source: \code{docs/34}, Section 5.2; verified except where marked
quoted).}
\label{tab:inve-schedule}
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{3.6cm}p{10.2cm}@{}}
\toprule
Feature & Effect \\
\midrule
More visits (first half on, one switch) & Lowers it: 0.605, 0.564, 0.508, 0.434 for 4, 8, 16, 32 visits at 1-week spacing; 0.758, 0.619, 0.480, 0.358 at 2.5-week spacing. Compound symmetry: 0.480, 0.346, 0.247, 0.176 \\
More on/off switches (16 visits, 8 on, equal blocks) & Lowers it sharply: 0.508, 0.358, 0.262, 0.149 for 1, 3, 7, 15 switches at 1-week spacing; 0.480, 0.428, 0.361, 0.278 at 2.5-week spacing \\
Shorter gap at the switch (8 visits, 4 on, study length 17.5 weeks) & Lowers it: 0.723, 0.619, 0.505, 0.331 at switch gaps of 8, 2.5, 1, 0.5 weeks \\
Uniform spacing (8 visits, 4 on) & Non-monotone: 0.564 at 1 week, 0.619 at 2.5, 0.494 at 10; at 0.5 weeks $M$ is not positive definite \\
More on-drug visits (8 visits, 2.5-week spacing, one switch) & Lowers it: 0.867, 0.758, 0.678, 0.619, 0.573, 0.536, 0.506 for 1 to 7 on \\
Clustering on-drug visits & Raises it: 0.735 with 1-week gaps on drug and 4-week gaps off, against 0.555 for the reverse (quoted) \\
Order of on-drug visits & Matters: 0.526, 0.497, 0.447 for three orders with equal counts (quoted) \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

From the 4-to-32-visit rows, the compound-symmetry ceiling falls by
$63\%$, the AR(1) ceiling by $28\%$ at 1-week and $53\%$ at 2.5-week
spacing, so the two do not fall in similar proportion (\code{docs/34},
Section 8; verified). The number of switches is the most consequential
decision, and its cost depends on spacing: going from one switch to
three costs $0.150$ at 1-week spacing and $0.052$ at 2.5-week spacing.
Crossing spacing and switch count for 16 visits, 8 on, reverses the
spacing preference: with one switch closer spacing is better ($0.524$
at 1.5 weeks against $0.397$ at 5), with fifteen it is worse ($0.149$
at 1 week against $0.331$ at 5) (verified; \code{docs/34}, Section 5.3,
reports that only 4 of its 24 grid designs can test $0.45$, quoted).
Replacing the paper~01 visit times by evenly spaced times over the same
span, keeping the on/off order, raises the ceiling from $0.465$ to
$0.507$ on Hybrid path C and from $0.452$ to $0.545$ on OL+BDC path A,
which is $0.093$ of the $0.167$ gap between OL+BDC and CO ($0.619$)
and $0.042$ of the $0.154$ gap for Hybrid path C (\code{docs/34},
Section 5.4; verified; Hybrid's binding path A was not equalized in the
source).

\textbf{A two-visit bound.} For unit-variance $B$, $BR_t$, $BR_s$ with
$\mathrm{Cor}(BR_t, BR_s) = A_{ts}$, the variable $BR_t - BR_s$ has
variance $2(1-A_{ts})$ and covariance $r_t - r_s$ with $B$, where
$r_t = c_{bm}g_t$; Cauchy--Schwarz gives the necessary condition
\begin{equation}
|r_t - r_s| \le \sqrt{2(1 - A_{ts})}
\label{eq:inve-pair}
\end{equation}
(Source: \code{docs/34}, Section 5.5; derived). The exact condition from
the $3\times3$ submatrix is
$(r_t^2 + r_s^2 - 2r_tr_sA_{ts})/(1 - A_{ts}^2) < 1$, which for
$r_s = 0$ reads $|r_t| < \sqrt{1 - A_{ts}^2}$, tighter than
Equation~\eqref{eq:inve-pair} ($0.714$ against $0.775$ at
$A_{ts} = 0.7$; verified). Either form shows why covariance moderation,
which needs a jump from $c_{bm}$ to near zero between adjacent on and
off visits, is constrained most where adjacent visits are most
correlated; the exact accounting is Equation~\eqref{eq:ce-steps}.

\subsection{Shape of the coupling decay}
\label{sec:inve-ce-shape}

If the off-drug coupling followed the Weibull family
(Equation~\eqref{eq:decay-weib}),
$g_t = \exp\{-(\lambda_w t_{sd,t})^k\}$ with
$\lambda_w = (\ln 2)^{1/k}/t_{1/2}$, rather than the exponential, the
shape would enter the ceiling only through the off-drug entries of
$g$; it does not affect $M$ and has no effect at $t_{1/2} = 0$, and
changing the shape of the carryover \emph{mean} alone leaves the
ceiling unchanged because the means do not enter $R$ (derived).
Table~\ref{tab:inve-weibull} gives the \code{covar} ceilings (Source:
\code{docs/34}, Section 5.6; verified). The package decays the
correlation exponentially only, so this is a counterfactual.

\begin{table}[h]
\centering\small
\caption{\code{covar} ceilings under Weibull-shaped coupling decay of
shape $k$, $\rho = 0.7$. Multi-cycle: 16 weekly visits, on, on, off,
off (Source: \code{docs/34}, Section 5.6; verified).}
\label{tab:inve-weibull}
\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccccc@{}}
\toprule
Design & $t_{1/2}$ & $k = 0.25$ & 0.5 & 1 (exp.) & 2 & 4 & No carryover \\
\midrule
Hybrid & 0.5 & 0.543 & 0.531 & 0.506 & 0.469 & 0.456 & 0.456 \\
Hybrid & 1   & 0.551 & 0.551 & 0.544 & 0.527 & 0.518 & \\
Hybrid & 2   & 0.556 & 0.563 & 0.563 & 0.551 & 0.529 & \\
OL+BDC & 0.5 & 0.524 & 0.518 & 0.500 & 0.465 & 0.452 & 0.452 \\
OL+BDC & 1   & 0.530 & 0.533 & 0.533 & 0.523 & 0.516 & \\
OL+BDC & 2   & 0.534 & 0.540 & 0.546 & 0.542 & 0.526 & \\
Multi-cycle & 0.5 & 0.390 & 0.353 & 0.301 & 0.271 & 0.262 & 0.262 \\
Multi-cycle & 1   & 0.415 & 0.400 & 0.364 & 0.308 & 0.292 & \\
Multi-cycle & 2   & 0.440 & 0.448 & 0.451 & 0.430 & 0.395 & \\
CO & any & 0.619 & 0.619 & 0.619 & 0.619 & 0.619 & 0.619 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

At $t_{1/2} = 0.5$ and $1$ the ceiling falls monotonically with $k$:
faster washout acts like less carryover, and at $k = 4$,
$t_{1/2} = 0.5$ the residual one week after stopping is
$1.5\times10^{-5}$, so Hybrid, OL+BDC and the multi-cycle design return
to their no-carryover ceilings. At $t_{1/2} = 2$ the ordering is not
monotone, since the off-drug visits fall one to three weeks after
discontinuation, where a small-$k$ curve lies below the exponential;
the residual at the visits actually sampled governs the ceiling. CO is
unaffected because its binding path at positive half-lives is the
placebo-first path. Because the no-carryover cell is the lowest across
shapes and half-lives, a target below it is feasible whatever the shape
(\code{docs/34}, Section 5.6; derived from the table).

\textbf{The step rule under underflow.} A Weibull curve never reaches
zero analytically, so for $k \le 2$ the step coupling is constant after
exposure and the published-specification ceiling at $\rho = 0.7$ is
$0.841$, as under the exponential. At $k = 4$ the residual underflows
to exactly zero in double precision ($8.6\times10^{-78}$ at two weeks
and $0$ at four weeks for $t_{1/2} = 0.5$), the step rule detects the
zero, and the ceiling falls to $0.384$ (Hybrid) and $0.467$ (OL+BDC) at
$t_{1/2} = 0.5$ and to $0.467$ (Hybrid) at $t_{1/2} = 1$ (Source:
\code{docs/34}, Section 5.6; verified). The ceiling of a step-coupled
process under fast washout is then set by floating-point arithmetic, not
by the model.

\subsection{The \code{covar} spectrum, a spacing condition, and the
published construct as two levels}
\label{sec:inve-ce-covar}

\textbf{Spectrum of \code{covar}.} $K_b = (c_1 - c_\times)(J_3 - I_3)$
has eigenvalues $2(c_1-c_\times)$ (once) and $-(c_1-c_\times)$ (twice),
and $K_a$ and $K_b$ share eigenvectors. Diagonalizing
$A = Q\,\mathrm{diag}(\omega_1,\ldots,\omega_n)Q^\top$ and permuting to
visit-major order turns $M_{\mathrm{covar}}$ into
$\bigoplus_j(\omega_jK_a + K_b)$, so its eigenvalues are
\begin{equation}
\omega_j(1 + 2c_\times) + 2(c_1 - c_\times), \qquad
\omega_j(1 - c_\times) - (c_1 - c_\times)\ \ (\text{twice}),
\qquad j = 1, \ldots, n,
\label{eq:inve-covar-eig}
\end{equation}
from which Equation~\eqref{eq:ce-covar-pd} follows (Source:
\code{docs/35}, Section 4.4; derived; verified to $4\times10^{-15}$ on
Hybrid path 1). The white-noise term of Section~\ref{sec:ce-sep} in
the summed response is
$s_t^\top K_b s_t = 2(c_1-c_\times)(s_{TV}s_{PB} + s_{TV}s_{BR} +
s_{PB}s_{BR}) > 0$ (derived).

\textbf{A sufficient spacing condition.} For equally spaced visits with
step correlation $\rho^d$, the eigenvalues of the AR(1)
Toeplitz matrix lie above the minimum of its spectral density,
$(1-\rho^d)/(1+\rho^d)$, and approach it as $n$ grows (Grenander and
Szeg\H{o}, 1958). Hence $M_{\mathrm{covar}} \succ 0$ for every $n$ if
\begin{equation}
\frac{1 - \rho^d}{1 + \rho^d} \ge \frac{c_1 - c_\times}{1 - c_\times}
\iff
d \ge \frac{\ln\{(1-k)/(1+k)\}}{\ln\rho}, \qquad
k = \frac{c_1 - c_\times}{1 - c_\times}
\label{eq:inve-gap}
\end{equation}
(Source: \code{docs/35}, Section 4.4; derived). At $\rho = 0.7$ this
requires gaps of at least $0.63$ weeks for $(c_1, c_\times) =
(0.2, 0.1)$, $0.70$ weeks for $(0.3, 0.2)$ and $0.30$ weeks for
$(0.1, 0.05)$; at $\rho = 0.8$ and $(0.2, 0.1)$ exactly one week
(verified). Consistently, on 16 visits at $\rho = 0.7$ the smallest
eigenvalue of $A$ is $0.090$ at half-week spacing, below the thresholds
$0.111$ and $0.125$ of the first two parameter sets, and $0.045$ at
quarter-week spacing, below the $0.053$ of the third (verified).
\note{\code{docs/35}, Section 4.4, gives $0.71$ weeks for $(0.3, 0.2)$;
the condition gives $\ln(7/9)/\ln 0.7 = 0.7046$, which rounds to
$0.70$.}

\textbf{The published construct as person and occasion levels.}
Equation~\eqref{eq:ce-cs-decomp} is the covariance of
$X_{c,t} = T_c + O_{c,t}$ with person-level effects
$T \sim \mathcal N(0, K_p)$ and occasion-level effects $O_t \sim \mathcal N(0, K_o)$
independent across visits (Diggle, 1988). The implied cross-component
correlations are
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{Cor}(T_c, T_{c'}) = \frac{c_\times}{\rho_{cs}}, \qquad
\mathrm{Cor}(O_{c,t}, O_{c',t}) = \frac{c_1 - c_\times}{1 - \rho_{cs}},
\label{eq:inve-orig-levels}
\end{equation}
$0.125$ and $0.500$ at the published $\rho_{cs} = 0.8$, $0.143$ and $0.333$
at $\rho_{cs} = 0.7$ (Source: \code{docs/35}, Section 4.5; derived;
verified). The published construct is thus a coherent two-level model
whose same-visit excess is carried by occasion-level variance; the
\code{covar} construct keeps the excess but has no occasion-level
variance to carry it, which is the indefinite $K_b$ term.

\textbf{One framework.} All three response blocks are instances of the
linear model of coregionalization, $M = \sum_j K_j \otimes A_j$ with
correlation kernels $A_j$, which is positive semidefinite whenever
every $K_j$ is (each term is then a Kronecker product of positive
semidefinite matrices). The published construct has two such terms,
the separable construct one, and \code{covar} one valid term and one
indefinite term (Source: \code{docs/35}, Section 4.7; derived). With
only two components the separable factor term becomes
$[K^{-1}]_{22} = 1/(1-c_1^2)$, $1.042$ at $c_1 = 0.2$ (\code{docs/35},
Section 7; derived); its consequence for the ceiling is
Equation~\eqref{eq:invc-sep-K}.

\subsection{A four-visit worked example of the factored ceiling}
\label{sec:inve-ce-example}

Take four visits of Hybrid path A at weeks $(10, 11, 12, 16)$, on, off,
off, on, with $\rho = 0.8$, $c_1 = 0.2$, $c_\times = 0.1$ and
$c_{bm} = 0.3$ (Source: \code{docs/36}, Appendices A.5 and A.7;
verified). The gaps are $(1, 1, 4)$, so
$\rho_t = (0.8, 0.8, 0.4096)$; $A$ has eigenvalues $2.693$, $0.825$,
$0.339$, $0.143$; and the smallest eigenvalue of the response block is
$0.100$ (published), $0.029$ (\code{covar}) and $0.115$ (separable).
For \code{covar}, $\omega_{\min}(A) = 0.143$ exceeds the threshold
$0.111$, and $0.143\times0.9 - 0.1 = 0.029$ is the predicted smallest
eigenvalue of Equation~\eqref{eq:inve-covar-eig}. With
$[K^{-1}]_{33} = 1.0714$, Equation~\eqref{eq:ce-steps} decomposes the
separable ceiling into per-step costs (Table~\ref{tab:inve-steps}).

\begin{table}[h]
\centering\small
\caption{Per-step decomposition of $g^\top A^{-1}g$ and the separable
ceiling, four-visit fragment (Source: \code{docs/36}, Appendix A.7.4;
verified).}
\label{tab:inve-steps}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lcccccc@{}}
\toprule
Coupling $g$ & $g_1^2$ & Step 1 & Step 2 & Step 3 & $g^\top A^{-1}g$ & $c_{bm}^{\ast}$ \\
\midrule
No carryover $(1, 0, 0, 1)$ & 1.000 & 1.778 & 0.000 & 1.202 & 3.979 & 0.4843 \\
Graded, $t_{1/2} = 1$: $(1, 0.5, 0.25, 1)$ & 1.000 & 0.250 & 0.063 & 0.968 & 2.281 & 0.6397 \\
Step, any carryover $(1, 1, 1, 1)$ & 1.000 & 0.111 & 0.111 & 0.419 & 1.641 & 0.7541 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

Without carryover the on-to-off step across one week costs
$\rho_t^2/(1-\rho_t^2) = 1.778$ and the off-to-on step across four weeks
$1/(1-\rho_t^2) = 1.202$; graded coupling softens both switches; the
constant step-rule pattern pays almost nothing and carries no contrast.
In the factored draw of Equation~\eqref{eq:ce-factored} for the graded
pattern, the conditional-mean weights
$c_{bm}[K^{-1}]_{c3}(A^{-1}g)_t$ on $BR$ are $0.5357$, $-0.1607$,
$-0.2759$, $0.3467$, those on $TV$ and $PB$ are $-1/6$ of them
($-0.0893$, $0.0268$, $0.0460$, $-0.0578$), removing the part of $BR$
shared with the other components, and the conditional variance is
$1 - c_{bm}^2\mathcal Q = 0.7801$, positive because $0.3 < 0.6397$ (verified).
The ratio $-1/6$ is $[K^{-1}]_{13}/[K^{-1}]_{33} = -c_1/(1+c_1)$
(derived).

\subsection{The number of response components and the ceiling}
\label{sec:invc-ce-K}

Papers~13 and~14 reduce the decomposition to two components (13 keeps
$BR + TV$, 14 keeps $BR + PB$). With $n_c$ components the joint
correlation matrix is $(2 + n_c n) \times (2 + n_c n)$: 26 square at
$n_c = 3$, $n = 8$, and 18 square at $n_c = 2$ (Source: \code{docs/31},
Sections 9 and 17; Paper~14, Appendix~B.6). Both papers expected the
reduction to relax the ceiling of Equation~\eqref{eq:ce-ceiling}. It
does not, and the reason is analytic.

\textbf{Compound symmetry with $n_c$ components.} With common
within-component correlation $\rho_{cs}$, same-visit cross-component
correlation $c_1$ and different-visit cross-component correlation
$c_\times$, the response block is
$M = A_{n_c} \otimes I_n + B_{n_c} \otimes (J_n - I_n)$ in occasion-major
order, with $A_{n_c} = I_{n_c} + c_1(J_{n_c} - I_{n_c})$ and
$B_{n_c} = \rho_{cs} I_{n_c} + c_\times(J_{n_c} - I_{n_c})$; this is
Equation~\eqref{eq:ce-cs-decomp} with $n_c$ in place of 3 and the
ordering of the Kronecker factors exchanged, which does not change
the spectrum. Its eigenvalues are those of $A_{n_c} + (n-1)B_{n_c}$ (once
each) and of $A_{n_c} - B_{n_c}$ (each with multiplicity $n - 1$), and both
are of the form $a I_{n_c} + b(J_{n_c} - I_{n_c})$, with eigenvalues $a + (n_c-1)b$
and $a - b$ (the latter $n_c - 1$ times). The four distinct eigenvalues
are
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
&\bigl[1 + (n-1)\rho_{cs}\bigr] + (n_c-1)\bigl[c_1 + (n-1)c_\times\bigr],
&\quad &\bigl[1 + (n-1)\rho_{cs}\bigr] - \bigl[c_1 + (n-1)c_\times\bigr],\\
&(1 - \rho_{cs}) + (n_c-1)(c_1 - c_\times),
&\quad &(1 - \rho_{cs}) - (c_1 - c_\times),
\end{aligned}
\label{eq:invc-cs-spectrum}
\end{equation}
the second and fourth present only when $n_c \ge 2$ (derived; Paper~14,
Appendix~C.2; verified for $n_c = 1$ to 4 and $n = 4, 8, 12$ at
$\rho_{cs} = 0.7$, $c_1 = 0.2$, $c_\times = 0.1$, \code{check-landmarks-hybrid-ceilings.R},
block 7). At those values and $n_c = 3$, $n = 8$ they are 7.7, 5.0, 0.5
and 0.2. When $n_c \ge 2$, $c_\times \le \rho_{cs}$ and $c_1 \ge c_\times$,
\begin{equation}
\omega_{\min}(M) = (1 - \rho_{cs}) - (c_1 - c_\times),
\label{eq:invc-lmin}
\end{equation}
which contains neither the number of visits nor the number of
components: removing a component does not move the positive-definiteness
boundary of the response block (Source: \code{docs/31}, Section 20;
Paper~14, Appendix~C.3; derived). As in \S\ref{sec:ce-sep},
$\omega_{\min}$ denotes the smallest eigenvalue. \note{2026-10-09:
notation. This subsection wrote the eigenvalue as $\lambda_{\min}$,
which collides with the carryover decay rate $\lambda$, and used $K$
both for the number of response components and for the
cross-component correlation matrix; the count is now $n_c$ and $K$ is
the matrix only.} Positive definiteness of the
block requires $\rho_{cs} < 1 - (c_1 - c_\times)$, that is $\rho_{cs} < 0.9$ at
the reference values. \note{\code{docs/31}, Section 20, states
Equation~\eqref{eq:invc-lmin} without conditions, and Paper~14 says
only that it is ``generally'' the smallest. Both conditions are needed:
with $c_\times > \rho_{cs}$ the second eigenvalue of
Equation~\eqref{eq:invc-cs-spectrum} is smaller (at $\rho_{cs} = 0.1$,
$c_1 = 0.2$, $c_\times = 0.15$, $n_c = 3$, $n = 8$ the smallest eigenvalue
is 0.45, not 0.85), and with $c_1 < c_\times$ the third is (at
$\rho_{cs} = 0.7$, $c_1 = 0.05$, $c_\times = 0.2$ it is 0, against 0.45 from
Equation~\eqref{eq:invc-lmin}); verified, block 7. With $n_c = 1$ the
smallest eigenvalue is $1 - \rho_{cs}$.}

\textbf{Numerical ceilings: three against two components.} The ceiling
itself depends on the coupling pattern and the schedule, not only on
$\omega_{\min}$. Under the package's non-separable AR(1) construct
$M_{\mathrm{covar}}$ (Equation~\eqref{eq:ce-covar-pd}; within-component
$\rho^{|w_t - w_s|}$, cross-component $c_1$ at the same visit and
$c_\times\rho^{|w_t - w_s|}$ otherwise) and graded coupling, the design
ceiling, minimized over paths, is given in
Table~\ref{tab:invc-ceil-K}; the three-component values are the
\code{covar} row of Table~\ref{tab:inve-crossed} and are not repeated
here. The two two-component reductions give the same matrix, since all
components share $\rho$ and the biomarker couples to $BR$ only.

\begin{table}[h]
\centering\small
\caption{Ceiling $c_{bm}^{\ast}$ under the non-separable AR(1) construct
with two components, and its gain over the three-component ceiling of
Table~\ref{tab:inve-crossed} (\code{covar} row), graded coupling,
$\rho = 0.7$, $c_1 = 0.2$, $c_\times = 0.1$, $n = 8$, minimum over paths
(Source: Paper~14, Appendix~C.4, quoted in \code{docs/31}, Section 20;
all entries verified, \code{check-landmarks-hybrid-ceilings.R}, block 8).}
\label{tab:invc-ceil-K}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}
\toprule
Design & $t_{1/2}$ & Two components & Gain \\
\midrule
CO & 0, 0.5, 1 & 0.627 & 0.008 \\
Hybrid & 0 & 0.469 & 0.013 \\
Hybrid & 0.5 & 0.516 & 0.011 \\
Hybrid & 1 & 0.552 & 0.008 \\
OL+BDC & 0 & 0.464 & 0.012 \\
OL+BDC & 0.5 & 0.509 & 0.009 \\
OL+BDC & 1 & 0.540 & 0.007 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\noindent The reduction buys between 0.007 and 0.013 of feasible
$c_{bm}$. \note{\code{docs/31}, Section 20, and Paper~14,
Appendix~C.4, give the range as 0.008 to 0.013. The unrounded gains run
from 0.0069 (OL+BDC, $t_{1/2} = 1$) to 0.0130 (Hybrid, $t_{1/2} = 0$),
and Paper~14's own rounded table gives $0.540 - 0.533 = 0.007$ for the
OL+BDC cell.} The ceilings rise with $t_{1/2}$ in Hybrid and OL+BDC and
are constant in CO, where the placebo-first path binds
(Section~\ref{sec:invc-spacing}). Paper~14 also
reports that, holding the design shape fixed, the three-component
ceiling falls from about 0.81 at $n = 4$ to 0.58 at $n = 12$, and that
the occasion-index exponent $\rho^{|j - j'|}$ in place of the week gap
lowers the worst-cell ceiling from about 0.45 to 0.35 \note{quoted;
not recomputed, because the ``fixed design shape'' at $n = 4$ and
$n = 12$ is not specified in the source}. These ceilings belong to
$M_{\mathrm{covar}}$ at $\rho = 0.7$ and are not comparable with the
compound-symmetry and separable entries of
Table~\ref{tab:ce-ceilings} (\code{docs/31}, Section 20).

\textbf{The separable form: a design-free ratio.} Under the separable
covariance of Equation~\eqref{eq:ce-sep} with $n_c$ equicorrelated
components, $K_{n_c} = (1 - c_1) I_{n_c} + c_1 J_{n_c}$ has
$[K_{n_c}^{-1}]_{n_c n_c} = \frac{1 + (n_c-2)c_1}{(1 - c_1)(1 + (n_c-1)c_1)}$, so
Equation~\eqref{eq:ce-factor} holds with
\begin{equation}
R_{n_c}^2 = \frac{(n_c - 1)\, c_1^2}{1 + (n_c - 2)\, c_1}, \qquad
\frac{c_{bm}^{\ast}(n_c = 2)}{c_{bm}^{\ast}(n_c = 3)} =
\sqrt{\frac{1 - R_2^2}{1 - R_3^2}} =
\sqrt{\frac{(1 - c_1^2)(1 + c_1)}{1 + c_1 - 2c_1^2}},
\label{eq:invc-sep-K}
\end{equation}
which reduces to Equation~\eqref{eq:ce-factor} at $n_c = 3$, and in which
the schedule, the coupling pattern and $\rho$ cancel (derived here, as
a corollary of Equation~\eqref{eq:ce-factor}; not stated in the
sources). The ratio is 1.0042, 1.0142 and 1.0277 at $c_1 = 0.1$, 0.2
and 0.3; at $\rho = 0.7$, $c_1 = 0.2$ and $t_{1/2} = 0$ the separable
design ceilings move from 0.616, 0.480 and 0.474 (CO, Hybrid, OL+BDC)
to 0.625, 0.487 and 0.481 (verified, block 9). The reduction's gain is
therefore at most a few percent under either construct, and
\code{docs/31}'s conclusion that the case for a reduction is
transparency and cost, not numerical headroom, holds under both.

\subsection{The outcome covariance: a random intercept plus AR(1)}

Let $s_t = (s_{TV,t}, s_{PB,t}, s_{BR,t})^{\top}$ be the component
standard deviations at visit $t$ and $S_t = \sum_c s_{c,t} X_{c,t}$
the summed response, so that $Y_{it} = \mathrm{BL}_i - S_{it}$ with
$\mathrm{BL}_i$ independent of the responses. Under
Equation~\eqref{eq:ce-sep},
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{Cov}(Y_{it}, Y_{is}) = \sigma_{BL}^2 + (s_t^{\top} K s_s)\,
  A_{ts},
\label{eq:ce-outcome-cov}
\end{equation}
which, when the component standard deviations are constant over
visits, is a random intercept plus an AR(1) process: the residual
structure of the random-intercept-plus-\code{corCAR1} analysis model
(Equation~\eqref{eq:common-analysis}), not of \code{corCAR1} alone.
The placebo-belief standard deviation scales with the visit's
expectancy weight $\eta_t$ (1 open label, 0.5 blinded), so the match is
close but not exact; \code{docs/35} reports a correlation factor of
0.976 between an open-label and a blinded visit, recomputed as 0.9755
in Equation~\eqref{eq:invd-q} of \S\ref{sec:invd-phase}. Under
$M_{\mathrm{covar}}$ the same calculation adds
$(s_t^{\top} K_b s_t)\,\mathbf 1[t = s]$, which is positive, a
white-noise term the analysis model omits. (Derived. Source:
\code{docs/35}, Section 4.6; Paper 01, Appendix A.5.) \note{The 0.976
factor is confirmed in \S\ref{sec:invd-phase} (0.9755, configuration C,
$\rho = 0.7$; verified, \code{check-stress-test-construct.R}, Section 5), which also
shows that a random intercept with phase-specific residual variances
reproduces every element of this covariance except the open-label by
blinded pairs. The effect of the white-noise term on test calibration
has not been measured in any source.}

\subsection{The generalized ceiling with prognostic and baseline
couplings}

When the biomarker is also coupled to $TV$, $PB$ or the baseline, write
its correlations with the three components over the visits as vectors
$\mathbf r_{TV}$, $\mathbf r_{PB}$, $\mathbf r_{BR}$ (with $\mathbf r_{BR} = c_{bm}\,g$) and its
correlation with $\mathrm{BL}$ as $c_{bm,BL}$. Because $\mathrm{BL}$ is
uncorrelated with the responses, the block of $R$ below the biomarker
row is $\mathrm{diag}(1, M)$, and the Schur complement with
$M^{-1} = K^{-1} \otimes A^{-1}$ gives
\begin{equation}
R \succ 0 \iff M \succ 0 \;\text{ and }\;
c_{bm,BL}^2 + \sum_{c, c'} [K^{-1}]_{cc'}\; \mathbf r_c^{\top} A^{-1} \mathbf r_{c'} < 1.
\label{eq:ce-general}
\end{equation}
Equation~\eqref{eq:ce-factor} is the special case $c_{bm,BL} = 0$,
$\mathbf r_{TV} = \mathbf r_{PB} = 0$. The off-diagonal elements of $K^{-1}$ are
negative, $-c_1/\bigl((1 - c_1)(1 + 2c_1)\bigr) = -0.179$ at
$c_1 = 0.2$, so same-sign couplings to positively correlated
components cost less than the sum of their separate costs. On Hybrid
path A at $\rho = 0.7$, $c_1 = 0.2$, coupling 0.3 to $BR$ alone costs
0.390, a constant prognostic coupling $\mathbf r_{TV} = 0.3\cdot\mathbf 1$
alone costs 0.342, and both together 0.663: the $BR$--$TV$ cross term
is $-0.070$. Adding $c_{bm,BL} = 0.3$ gives 0.7525, and the full matrix
gives the same value, so these stress-test cells are feasible.
(Derived; verified, \code{check-ceilings.R}. Source: \code{docs/35},
Section 4.8.) \note{\code{docs/35}, Section 4.8, does not state $\rho$
for these figures; they reproduce at $\rho = 0.7$.} A biomarker that
is predictive and prognostic in the same direction is therefore easier
to accommodate than either coupling alone suggests.

\subsection{Silent repair, and why the generator must stop}
\label{sec:ce-repair}

The published driver calls \code{generateData()} with
\code{makePositiveDefinite = TRUE}, and a matrix that fails the check
\code{corpcor::is.positive.definite()} is replaced, without notice, by
\code{make.positive.definite(sigma, tol = 1e-3)}. At
$\rho = 0.8$ and $c_{bm} = 0.6$ the repair lowers the on-drug
biomarker--response correlation to 0.540--0.568, introduces 0.029--0.042
at off-drug visits, and moves other correlations by up to 0.097; at
$c_{bm} = 0.3$ the largest change is 0.007 (\code{docs/36}, Section
4.3). The package keeps two repairs: \code{buildSigma()} repairs under
the same flag and warns only when \code{verbose = TRUE}, and the
Cholesky step falls back to a repaired matrix whether or not the flag
is set (\code{R/generateData.R}, lines 377--388 and 391--393; inspected
2026-10-08). Just above the ceiling the perturbation is at most 0.004
(\code{docs/34}, Section 4.7, at $\rho = 0.7$), small enough to pass
unnoticed.

Stopping is required, not merely preferable, for three reasons. Above
$c_{bm}^{\ast}$ no joint distribution with the stated correlations
exists, so a repaired matrix is a different model, and the power
reported for the requested $c_{bm}$ belongs to a data-generating
process that was never stated. The repair changes the effect in a
direction and amount that depend on design, path and $c_{bm}$, and it
can move coupling into off-drug visits, so the resulting bias cannot
be undone after the fact. And because the step rule makes exactly the
signal-free cells feasible (Section~\ref{sec:ce-cs}), repair counts
and power together can describe a construct quite unlike the one in
the Methods. Paper~01's refinement 1 (Appendix B.2) replaces the repair
by a \code{stop()}. Under the separable structure the condition is
part of generation itself: drawing three AR(1) recursions, mixing them
by $L_K$, and drawing the biomarker from
\begin{equation}
B_i \mid \mathbf X \sim \mathcal N\Bigl(c_{bm} \sum_{c} [K^{-1}]_{c3}\,
  X_c^{\top} A^{-1} g,\;\; 1 - c_{bm}^2\, \mathcal Q\Bigr), \qquad
\mathcal Q = [K^{-1}]_{33}\, g^{\top} A^{-1} g,
\label{eq:ce-factored}
\end{equation}
gives a conditional variance that is positive exactly when
$c_{bm} < \mathcal Q^{-1/2} = c_{bm}^{\ast}$, so an infeasible effect size
cannot be simulated. (Derived; the generator was checked against its
target distribution in \code{docs/36}, Section 6.7, where a request of
$c_{bm} = 0.6$ against the ceiling of Hybrid path A under graded
coupling at $t_{1/2} = 0.5$, separable, $\rho = 0.8$, is refused; that
ceiling is 0.491, 0.490934, as in Table~\ref{tab:inve-config}.)
\note{2026-10-08: corrected from 0.494. \code{docs/36}, Section 6.7, and
its check script, \code{07-factored-generator.R} in
\code{analysis/scripts/quick-sim/cbm-ceiling/} (line 75), enter the
coupling at visit 8 as $0.0625$; that visit has $t_{sd} = 4$, so graded
coupling at $t_{1/2} = 0.5$ gives $2^{-8} = 0.0039$. With the source's
vector the ceiling is $0.494329$, as reported; with the correct vector
it is $0.490934$ (verified, \code{check-closed-form-extensions.R}; the same value is in
\code{design-power.csv}). The generator check itself (closed form
against full matrix, sampled against target correlations) is
unaffected, since it is internally consistent for whatever vector is
supplied, and the refusal of $c_{bm} = 0.6$ stands.}
\note{The factored generator and a pre-simulation ceiling check exist
only in the simulation drivers. As of \code{docs/35}, Section 10, and
the inspection above, the package's sampler still repairs silently;
steps 1 to 3 of the \code{docs/35} implementation plan are pending.}

\subsection{The 2024 revision of the published code: which change fixes
the ceiling}
\label{sec:invd-ce-2024-fix}

The 2024 code couples the biomarker by the ratio-graded rule of
Equation~\eqref{eq:invd-ratio-coupling} and leaves the first visit
uncoupled (\S\ref{sec:invd-ce-2024}).
\textbf{The crossed decomposition.} The source builds one correlation
matrix with four switches that turn the 2024 construct into the
package's \code{covar} construct: \code{ar1} (AR(1) within components
instead of compound symmetry), \code{cfd} (cross-component block
$c_\times\rho^{|w_t-w_s|}$ instead of the constant $c_\times$),
\code{bexp} (graded coupling instead of
Equation~\eqref{eq:invd-ratio-coupling}) and \code{occ1} (the first
visit coupled). Table~\ref{tab:invd-decomp} gives the exact worst-cell
ceiling of each of the 16 combinations over three designs and
$t_{1/2} \in \{0, 0.5, 1\}$ at $\rho = 0.7$, $c_1 = 0.2$,
$c_\times = 0.1$, and Table~\ref{tab:invd-decomp-design} the cells for
four of them; its \code{covar} column is the first row of
Table~\ref{tab:inve-crossed} to four decimals, repeated as the
reference for the comparison. (Source: \code{docs/54}, Section 5,
on a grid of step 0.01; recomputed exactly here, and every grid value
of the source's Tables 5.2 and 5.3 is the floor of the exact value,
\code{check-stress-test-construct.R}, Section 2.)
