\noindent Under CS there is no interior optimum (the information is
monotone in $d$), and the 95\% spacings are four to five half-lives for
the alternating design at the shorter half-lives, about 2.4 to 4 for
ABAB, and about two for CO, whose placebo-first sequence carries no
carryover. Under AR(1) the optimum is interior and moves out with the
measurement-error share: about two half-lives without it, three to four
at $\omega = 0.2$, four and a half to six at $\omega = 0.5$. Without
measurement error the exact grid optima, 0.50, 1.00, 2.00 and 4.25 weeks,
agree with the maxima of Equation~\eqref{eq:bp-spacing}, 0.45, 0.91, 1.88
and 4.34, to the 0.25-week grid resolution (verified), which settles the
point left open in the note after Equation~\eqref{eq:bp-spacing}. ABAB
optima are shorter because the second visit of each off-drug block is
already two spacings after discontinuation (argued).

\subsection{A closed-form screen from visit schedules}

Paper 12 approximates E1 under mean moderation by the same two-group
contrast and writes, for a participant with $k_{\text{on}}$ and
$k_{\text{off}}$ visits at fixed times,
\begin{equation}
\beta_{bm:D} = \beta_D\,\beta_{bm}\,A, \qquad A = 1 - \bar D^{\,\text{off}}_{bc},
\qquad
\operatorname{Var}(\hat\beta_{bm:D}) \approx \frac{\sigma^2 v_1}{N},
\quad
v_1 = \frac{\mathbf 1'R_{\text{on}}\mathbf 1}{k_{\text{on}}^2}
  + \frac{\mathbf 1'R_{\text{off}}\mathbf 1}{k_{\text{off}}^2},
\label{eq:bp-paper12}
\end{equation}
with power by the Wald-normal approximation (Paper 12, Section 3).
\note{The source writes the drug effect as $\beta_1$ (here $\beta_D$)
and the moderation as $\beta_1(1 + \beta_{bm}B_i)$,
a multiplicative form on the drug effect, rather than the canonical
additive shift $\beta_{bm}\sigma_{BR}b_i$, and uses $\gamma_0$ and
$\eta_i$ for the intercept and residual; $\eta$ is the expectancy here.}
\note{Correction. $v_1$ omits the cross term: the exact time part is
$v_1 - 2\,\mathbf 1'R_{\text{on,off}}\mathbf 1/(k_{\text{on}}
k_{\text{off}})$, as in \eqref{eq:bp-master}. Under positive serial
correlation the approximation always overstates the variance, and most
for interleaved designs. At $\rho = 0.7$ it gives 0.909 against an
exact 0.770 for the 2.5-week crossover, but 0.926 against 0.464 and
0.910 against 0.451 for Hybrid paths 1 and 3 (verified,
\code{check-design.R}). This accounts for the source's residual
pattern (crossover under-predicted, Hybrid and OL+BDC over-predicted
after one common $\sigma^2$ is fitted), which it attributes to the same
approximation without quantifying it. Since the exact form costs no
more to evaluate, the screen should use it.} The source also reports
that $A(t_{1/2})$ ranks designs correctly but overstates the attenuation
of the fitted coefficient, and recommends it only as a relative screen
(quoted).

\subsection{Separation of the biomarker block}
\label{sec:bp-sep}

Let the analysis include
the biomarker only through the columns $B_i\,\mathbf z_t$, where
$\mathbf z_t$ collects the regressors it multiplies (for the strawman
analysis S of Table~\ref{tab:invd-analyses},
$\mathbf z_t = (1, D_{bc,it})$). Because $B_i$ is centered and
independent of path, its cross-products with every column not
multiplied by $B_i$ (intercept, $f(t)$, $D_{bc}$, $\eta_t$) vanish in
the probability limit, and the GLS normal equations separate. With
$Z_p$ the $9 \times q$ matrix of $\mathbf z_t$ on path $p$, $V_p$ its
working covariance, $N_p$ its allocation and $\mathbf s_p$ the true
slope vector,
\begin{equation}
\operatorname{plim}\,\hat{\boldsymbol\beta}_{\text{bm block}}
  = \Big(\sum_p N_p\, Z_p'V_p^{-1}Z_p\Big)^{-1}
    \sum_p N_p\, Z_p'V_p^{-1}\mathbf s_p
\label{eq:bp-plim}
\end{equation}
(derived here). Two consequences follow. The probability limit of
$\beta_{bm:D}$ does not depend on the time adjustment $f(t)$, which
enters only the other block (consistent with the agreement to 0.0009
across time adjustments in \code{docs/42}, Section 4). And if
$\mathbf s_p$ lies in the column space of $Z_p$ on every path, the fit
is exact for \emph{any} working covariance.

\subsection{The most efficient contrast}
\label{sec:invf-optcontrast}

Under mean moderation with profile $\mathbf h = (h_t)$ on a path whose
response covariance is $C$, a contrast $\mathbf a$ has slope
$-\beta_{bm}(\sigma_{BR}/\sigma_{bm})\,\mathbf a'\mathbf h$ and residual
variance $\mathbf a'C\mathbf a$, so the squared noncentrality is
proportional to $(\mathbf a'\mathbf h)^2/\mathbf a'C\mathbf a$
(Equation~\eqref{eq:cg-ncp} with $r^2_{\Delta B}/(1 - r^2_{\Delta B})$).
Among contrasts with $\mathbf 1'\mathbf a = 0$, which cancel the baseline
level and any biomarker main effect, the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality in the
$C$ inner product gives the maximizer and the maximum
\begin{equation}
\mathbf a^{\ast} \propto C^{-1}\big(\mathbf h - \bar h_C\mathbf 1\big),
\quad \bar h_C = \frac{\mathbf 1'C^{-1}\mathbf h}{\mathbf 1'C^{-1}\mathbf 1},
\qquad
\max_{\mathbf 1'\mathbf a = 0}\frac{(\mathbf a'\mathbf h)^2}{\mathbf a'C\mathbf a}
  = \mathbf h'C^{-1}\mathbf h - \frac{(\mathbf 1'C^{-1}\mathbf h)^2}
    {\mathbf 1'C^{-1}\mathbf 1},
\label{eq:invf-optcontrast}
\end{equation}
which is the reciprocal of the GLS variance of the coefficient of
$B_i h_t$ in the biomarker block $(B_i, B_i h_t)$ with $V = C$
(Equation~\eqref{eq:bp-plim}): a correctly specified mixed model attains
it (derived; Source: \code{docs/39}, Section 10, item 6). The E9 weights
attain it only when $C^{-1}(\mathbf h - \bar h_C\mathbf 1)$ is
proportional to the on/off weights. Relative efficiencies of E9
(Table~\ref{tab:invf-opt}) were computed with the GLS form, which
matches Equation~\eqref{eq:invf-optcontrast} to machine precision, and
checked against 20,000 random sum-zero contrasts, none of which exceeds
it (verified, \code{check-baseline-design-search.R}).

\begin{table}[h]
\centering\small
\caption{Information of the E9 contrast relative to the optimum of
Equation~\eqref{eq:invf-optcontrast}, one path (verified).}
\label{tab:invf-opt}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lllr@{}}
\toprule
Design, path & Structure & $t_{1/2}$ & E9 / optimum \\
\midrule
Hybrid, 1 & CS & 0 & 0.950 \\
Hybrid, 1 & AR(1), $\omega = 0.2$ & 0 & 0.775 \\
Alternating, 2.5-week spacing & AR(1), $\omega = 0.2$ & 1 & 0.958 \\
CO, 2.5-week spacing & AR(1), $\omega = 0.2$ & 1 & 0.922 \\
CO, 2.5-week spacing & CS & 1 & 0.989 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\noindent Equal weighting loses most where the design is irregular and
the correlation decays (Hybrid under AR(1): 22.5\% of the information).
\note{\code{docs/39}, Section 10, item 6, states that the best contrast
is proportional to $C^{-1}$ times the moderation pattern; among
contrasts invariant to a biomarker main effect it is
$C^{-1}(\mathbf h - \bar h_C\mathbf 1)$, the projection that
Equation~\eqref{eq:invf-optcontrast} makes explicit.}

\subsection{Time adjustment and the probability limit of the drug
coefficient}

The time term $f(t)$ must absorb the non-drug mean, which in the Hybrid
design is common to all paths: a sigmoid natural course plus an
expectancy curve that halves at week 9. Under working independence,
the probability limit of the drug coefficient is the allocation-weighted
least-squares fit to the population means, $\operatorname{plim}
\hat\beta_D = \big[(X'WX)^{-1}X'W\boldsymbol\mu\big]_D$. With visit
effects, Frisch-Waugh-Lovell gives
\begin{equation}
\operatorname{plim}\hat\beta_D
  = \frac{\sum_t\sum_p N_p\,(D_{pt} - \bar D_t)\,\mu_{pt}}
         {\sum_t\sum_p N_p\,(D_{pt} - \bar D_t)^2},
  \qquad \bar D_t = \frac{\sum_p N_p D_{pt}}{\sum_p N_p},
\label{eq:bp-fwl}
\end{equation}
so only visits at which paths differ in drug state contribute, weeks
10, 16 and 20 in the Hybrid design (derived; \code{docs/42}, Section 3,
states the identification in words).

\begin{table}[h]
\centering\small
\caption{Probability limit of $\beta_D$ under working independence, by
time adjustment, Hybrid, graded coupling (Source: \code{docs/42},
Section 4; values inspected in \code{time-adjustment-plim.csv}).}
\label{tab:bp-plim}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lrrr@{}}
\toprule
Time adjustment & RMS misfit, $t_{1/2} = 0$ & $t_{1/2} = 0$ & $t_{1/2} = 1$ \\
\midrule
Linear $t$ & 3.18 & $-8.21$ & $-6.38$ \\
Linear $t$ + $\eta_t$ & 3.11 & $-7.28$ & $-4.81$ \\
Natural spline, 3 df & 1.93 & $-6.22$ & $-3.67$ \\
Visit factor & 0.80 & $-6.25$ & $-4.49$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\noindent Linear time credits about 2 points of open-label expectancy to
the drug; visit effects remove the confounding at about 1.5 times the
standard deviation (0.86 against 1.27, quoted). By the separation
argument of \eqref{eq:bp-plim}, the probability limit of
$\beta_{bm:D}$ is the same under every $f(t)$ when the biomarker is
unrelated to the non-drug components. \note{These limits are for an
independence working model; the random-intercept GLS of the fitted
model converges elsewhere, which is why the simulated means ($-8.16$,
$-6.02$, $-5.99$ at $t_{1/2} = 0$) differ from them, most for the
flexible forms. The source script labels them illustrations.} The size
excess of the flexible adjustments in the Hybrid carryover cell (0.075
spline and 0.080 visit factor against 0.056 linear under model-based
inference; reproduced in \code{docs/45}, Section 4.4, and absent under
CR2) is a property of inference, not of these limits; its mechanism is
not established (quoted).

\subsection{The non-drug mean of the Hybrid design}
\label{sec:invf-means}

In the published process the natural course follows the modified
Gompertz curve in calendar weeks, $E(TV_t) = G(w_t; 6.506, 5, 0.35)$
(Equation~\eqref{eq:gompertz-05}), the placebo response the same curve
scaled by expectancy, $E(PB_t) = \eta_t\,G(w_t; 6.506, 5, 0.35)$
(its clock $t_{pb}$ equals $w_t$ after baseline), and the drug response
$E(BR_t) = G(t_{od}; 10.986, 5, 0.42)$ on drug. The non-drug mean is
common to all paths; its values by week, with the drug-response means
of every path, are in Table~\ref{tab:invc-means}
(\S\ref{sec:invc-hybrid-construct}; Source: \code{docs/42}, Section 2;
verified). The non-drug mean rises to week 8, falls by 1.71 at week 9 when
expectancy halves, and then flattens, so no line follows it. At weeks 4,
8 and 9 every path is on drug and the drug response rises from 4.28 to
9.79 (10.19 at week 10), variation that a single $D$ coefficient cannot
represent and that a flexible time term absorbs; this is why the time
adjustment moves $\operatorname{plim}\hat\beta_D$ so much
(Table~\ref{tab:bp-plim}). \note{\code{docs/42} gives $TV + PB = 3.72$
at week 4, the sum of the rounded components; the exact value is
3.73.} The collinearity of the expectancy regressor with the drug
indicator, which \code{docs/42}, Section 7, quotes from \code{docs/40}
as 0.59 and 1.5, is reconciled in \S\ref{sec:invc-hybrid-construct}.

\subsection{Cycle count and period length}
\label{sec:invb-cycles}

\code{docs/27} plans a sweep (block S13 of Paper 05) over designs
built by \code{build\_cycle\_design()}: an open-label run of
$T_{\mathrm{ol}}$ weeks on drug ($\eta = 1$), then $k$ blinded cycles
of $T_{\mathrm{on}}$ weeks on drug and $T_{\mathrm{off}}$ weeks off
($\eta = 0.5$), one observation per week, a single path in the
\code{on\_first} order (\code{analysis/\allowbreak scripts/\allowbreak
nof1-design-sensitivity/\allowbreak 00-common.R}, inspected). Optional
run-in and run-out add $T_{\mathrm{ri}}$ and $T_{\mathrm{ro}}$ off-drug
weeks, so the duration is $T_{\mathrm{ri}} + T_{\mathrm{ol}} +
k(T_{\mathrm{on}} + T_{\mathrm{off}}) + T_{\mathrm{ro}}$.

\textbf{Information on the interaction.} Take mean moderation without
carryover ($D_{bc} = D$), independent within-participant errors of
variance $\sigma^2$ with any random intercept, every participant on
the same schedule, and the analysis model
Equation~\eqref{eq:common-analysis}. Centering the biomarker, the
\code{bm:Dbc} column is orthogonal to every column that does not
involve the biomarker, and of the biomarker columns the model contains
only the main effect, so only the person-level mean of $D$ is
partialled out of it. Hence
\begin{equation}
\operatorname{Var}(\hat\beta_{bm:D}) = \frac{\sigma^2}
  {(N-1)s_b^2}\,\frac{n_{\mathrm{on}} + n_{\mathrm{off}}}
  {n_{\mathrm{on}}\, n_{\mathrm{off}}},
  \qquad n_{\mathrm{on}} = T_{\mathrm{ol}} + kT_{\mathrm{on}},
  \quad n_{\mathrm{off}} = T_{\mathrm{ri}} + kT_{\mathrm{off}} +
  T_{\mathrm{ro}},
\label{eq:invb-cycle-var}
\end{equation}
the special case of Equation~\eqref{eq:bp-vcs} with independent
within-participant errors (derived). Adding a linear time term or an
expectancy main effect $\eta_t$ leaves it unchanged; adding a
biomarker-by-expectancy term $b_i\eta_t$ (as in
Equation~\eqref{eq:bp-threephase}) removes the open-label visits, so
$n_{\mathrm{on}} = kT_{\mathrm{on}}$ (derived; all three verified to
machine precision for every Tier 1 cycle structure from the GLS
information of the full design matrix with a random intercept,
\code{check-gompertz-rmanova-cycles.R}). Three statements follow.

\begin{enumerate}[itemsep=2pt]
\item \textbf{Leading order.} Without open-label, run-in or run-out
  visits the information is
  \[
  I(\beta_{bm:D}) = \frac{(N-1)s_b^2}{\sigma^2}\;
    k\,\frac{T_{\mathrm{on}}T_{\mathrm{off}}}
    {T_{\mathrm{on}} + T_{\mathrm{off}}},
  \]
  exactly linear in $k$, which is
  \code{docs/27}'s Section 4 formula (Source: \code{docs/27},
  Section 4; derived). The factor $T_{\mathrm{on}}T_{\mathrm{off}}/
  (T_{\mathrm{on}} + T_{\mathrm{off}})$ is half the harmonic mean of
  the two period lengths.
\item \textbf{The open-label phase counts.} Under the analysis model
  the open-label weeks enter $n_{\mathrm{on}}$ directly. With
  $T_{\mathrm{ol}} = 8$ and $k = 2$, $T_{\mathrm{on}} =
  T_{\mathrm{off}} = 4$, the information per unit $(N-1)s_b^2/\sigma^2$
  is 5.33 against the leading-order 4.00 (verified). Linearity in $k$
  then fails: at $T_{\mathrm{on}} = T_{\mathrm{off}} = 2$ it is 1.67,
  3.00, 4.20 and 5.33 for $k = 1$ to 4.
\item \textbf{The symmetric optimum moves.} At a fixed cycle length
  $S = T_{\mathrm{on}} + T_{\mathrm{off}}$ the information is
  maximized at $T_{\mathrm{on}}^\ast = S/2 - (T_{\mathrm{ol}} -
  T_{\mathrm{ri}} - T_{\mathrm{ro}})/(2k)$, truncated to $[0, S]$
  (derived, maximizing $n_{\mathrm{on}}n_{\mathrm{off}}$ at fixed
  total). For the Tier 2a sweep ($k = 2$, $S = 8$, $T_{\mathrm{ol}} =
  8$) this is $T_{\mathrm{on}}^\ast = 2$, not 4: the information is
  6.00, 5.83, 5.33, 4.50 and 3.33 at $T_{\mathrm{on}} = 2, \ldots, 6$.
  With a $b_i\eta_t$ term in the model it is 3.00, 3.75, 4.00, 3.75 and
  3.00, symmetric about 4 (verified).
\end{enumerate}

\note{\code{docs/27} (Section 2a) argues that open-label observations
contribute to the interaction only indirectly, ``their $D_{bc,it} = 1$
value is identical to the within-cycle on-drug observations, so they
do not enlarge the on/off contrast'', and (Sections 3b and 4) predicts
a symmetric optimum at $(4, 4)$ without carryover. Both hold only for
an analysis that includes a biomarker-by-expectancy (or
biomarker-by-phase) term. Under the planned E2 analysis, which has
none, the open-label weeks are on-drug observations of the contrast,
and the no-carryover optimum of the Tier 2a sweep lies at the
shortest on-drug period. The open-label visits also carry
$\eta_t = 1$, so under a $PB$-coupled biomarker they are the visits
through which expectancy confounding enters (\S\ref{sec:bp-design},
Table~\ref{tab:invf-parts}). Because the S13 designs have a single path,
drug state is partly confounded with time, which costs information on
$\beta_D$ when a time term is fitted; it does not affect
$\beta_{bm:D}$, by the orthogonality above (derived). AR(1) errors
replace Equation~\eqref{eq:invb-cycle-var} by
Equation~\eqref{eq:bp-var}.}

\textbf{Tier 1 grid size.} With $T_{\mathrm{ri}} = T_{\mathrm{ro}} =
0$, $T_{\mathrm{ol}} = 8$ and a 32-week cap, the constraint is
$k(T_{\mathrm{on}} + T_{\mathrm{off}}) \le 24$ over $k \in \{1, 2, 3,
4\}$ and $T_{\mathrm{on}}, T_{\mathrm{off}} \in \{2, 4, 6\}$. It keeps
all nine period pairs at $k = 1$ and 2, six at $k = 3$ and three at
$k = 4$: 27 cycle structures, hence $27 \times 12 = 324$ planned cells
over two architectures, three half-lives and two values of $c_{bm}$,
and $27 \times 30 = 810$ cells in the driver, which uses five values of
$c_{bm}$ (derived; verified by enumeration, \code{check-gompertz-rmanova-cycles.R};
Source: \code{docs/27}, Section 3a and status note).

%#############################################################
\part{Analysis}
%#############################################################

%=============================================================
\section{The analysis model and its carryover specifications}
\label{sec:analysis}
%=============================================================

This section states the analysis model common to the compendium, its
open-label branch, and the carryover specifications of Paper 02,
followed by two historical analyses of carryover.

\subsection{The common analysis model}

Across the compendium both architectures are fit identically:
\begin{equation}
\code{Sx \textasciitilde\ bm + t + Dbc + bm:Dbc}
\label{eq:common-analysis}
\end{equation}
via \code{nlme::lme} with \code{corCAR1(form = \textasciitilde
t|ptID)} residual correlation and a participant random intercept. The
inferential machinery of \S\ref{sec:inference} does not know which
architecture generated the data it is fitting.

The restated Paper 01 departs from this convention in two places. The
published analysis it reproduces is the \code{lmer} model $Y_{it} =
\beta_0 + \beta_B B_i + \beta_D D_{it} + \beta_T t + \beta_{bm:D} B_i
D_{it} + u_i + \varepsilon_{it}$, with the \emph{binary} $D_{it}$ and
baseline as a row with $D_{i0} = 0$; under carryover a binary
indicator estimates an attenuated on-minus-off contrast, so part of
the power loss reflects the analysis, not the data-generating process
(Paper 01, Estimand). Its stress test compares fourteen analyses under
configuration C at $\rho = 0.7$, the covariance construct of the
refined process, with the published recursive carryover
(\S\ref{sec:invd-dgp}), and selects a random-intercept model with
continuous-time AR(1) residuals, expectancy terms and CR2 standard
errors (Paper 01, Results; \code{docs/46}, \code{docs/47}). That
selection comes from an exploratory comparison. (Updated 2026-10-15: a
rerun under the refined process, with anchored carryover, randomized
path allocation and, post hoc, 1,500 further trials per cell for the
eight surviving analyses, retains it; its two phase-variance versions
G1 and G2 tie with it within the margin of the selection rule,
\code{docs/47}.)

\note{2026-10-08: superseded. A note here previously stated that Paper
01's Methods give $\theta_{\mathrm{true}} = -c_{bm}\sigma_{BR}$ and that
this is consistent with mean estimates of $-0.23$ to $-0.28$. The
restated Paper 01 gives the estimand as $-c_{bm}\sigma_{BR}/\sigma_{bm}$
(covariance) and $-\beta_{bm}\sigma_{BR}/\sigma_{bm}$ (mean), which
reduces to $-c_{bm}\sigma_{BR}$ only when $\sigma_{bm} = 1$, as in the
Paper 02 driver (Equation~\eqref{eq:theta-true}). The consistency claim
was not supported: with $c_{bm} = 0.45$ it would require $\sigma_{BR}
\approx 0.5$ to $0.6$, and it is withdrawn.}

\subsection{The open-label branch of the analysis model and the
biomarker threshold}
\label{sec:inva-olbranch}

\textbf{Open-label branch.} When no participant's drug state varies
after baseline, \code{lme\_analysis()} replaces the exposure terms of
Equation~\eqref{eq:common-analysis} by a biomarker-by-time interaction,
\begin{equation}
Y_{it} = \beta_0 + \beta_B B_i + \beta_T t + \beta_{bm:t}\,B_i t
  + u_i + \varepsilon_{it},
\label{eq:inva-ol}
\end{equation}
with a random intercept, \code{corCAR1} residuals and the raw
biomarker (code \code{Sx \textasciitilde\ bm + t + bm:t};
\code{R/lme\_analysis.R}, inspected; Source: \code{docs/23},
Section~4.1). The coefficient $\beta_{bm:t}$ moderates the whole
trajectory, whose slope contains $BR$, $PB$ and $TV$, so it is not the
drug-specific estimand $\beta_{bm:D}$; and its within-participant
information is $\sum_t (t - \bar t_i)^2$ rather than the exposure
information of \S\ref{sec:inva-ssw} (\code{docs/23}, Section~13.3;
argued). \note{The source writes $\beta_1$, $\beta_2$, $\beta_3$ for
$\beta_B$, $\beta_T$, $\beta_{bm:t}$. Under both architectures as
implemented the biomarker-dependent part of the mean is constant over
the post-baseline open-label visits (constant shift, or $g_t = 1$), and
zero at the baseline row, so it reaches $\beta_{bm:t}$ only through the
projection of that step onto linear time (argued). This is consistent
with the near-nominal open-label power quoted in \code{docs/23},
Section~6.3 (0.06 to 0.10), but was not derived.}

\textbf{Biomarker threshold.} With the raw biomarker in the model, the
fitted drug effect at biomarker value $B$ is
$\hat\beta_D + \hat\beta_{bm:D}B$, so the biomarker at which it equals a
clinical threshold $y^\ast$ is
\begin{equation}
B_{\text{thr}} = \frac{y^\ast - \hat\beta_D}{\hat\beta_{bm:D}},
\qquad\text{or}\qquad
B_{\text{thr}} = \bar B + \frac{y^\ast - \hat\beta_D^{c}}{\hat\beta_{bm:D}}
\ \text{if the biomarker is centered at } \bar B \text{ before the fit},
\label{eq:inva-threshold}
\end{equation}
with $\hat\beta_D^{c}$ the drug coefficient of the centered fit
(derived). \note{\code{docs/23}, Section~9.1, flags that
\code{analyze\_trial\_extended()} combined the uncentered coefficient
with the $\bar B$ offset. The working-tree \code{R/carryover\_analysis.R}
now computes the first form and its \code{predicted\_effect} as
$\hat\beta_D + \hat\beta_{bm:D}B$ (inspected 2026-10-08; the change is
uncommitted).}

\subsection{Analysis specifications for carryover: E1--E9}
\label{sec:e-specs}

Paper 02 fixes the analysis model's carryover treatment across nine
specifications, all fit as variants of
\begin{equation}
\code{nlme::lme(Sx \textasciitilde\ bm * X + t, random = \textasciitilde 1 | ptID,
  correlation = corCAR1(form = \textasciitilde t | ptID))}
\label{eq:e-base-model}
\end{equation}
differing only in the construction of the exposure predictor $X$ and,
for the CR-variant rows, the standard-error estimator
(\S\ref{sec:sandwich}). Table~\ref{tab:especs} reproduces the full
specification table under the stored codes.

\textbf{Labels.} \code{NOTATION.md} (Part 3) defines E1, E2 and E3
and, since 2026-10-09, lists the extended E series (E4 to E7, E9, E9b
and the CR2 variants) so that no label is reused; it requires stored
and reported labels to be identical. The further codes E7, E9 and the
CR2 variants are the values stored in Paper 02's data files; Paper 02 now displays all nine as G1--G9 (or
by name) through a render-time mapping in \code{spec-labels.R}, which
departs from that rule. Paper 01 uses E9 for the paired-difference
statistic and E9b for the same statistic with baseline counted as an
off-drug visit. This document uses the stored E codes.

\begin{table}[h]
\centering\small
\caption{The nine carryover analysis specifications (Paper 02).}
\label{tab:especs}
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{2.6cm}p{2.4cm}p{6.4cm}p{2.0cm}@{}}
\toprule
Stored (Paper 02 display) & Name & $X$ / model & Target \\
\midrule
E1 (G1) & Unadjusted & $X = D_{it}$ (binary), no carryover term & $bm{:}D_{it}$ \\
E3 (G2) & Lag-adjusted & $X = D_{it}$, plus nuisance $L_{it} = D_{i,t-1}(1-D_{it})$ & $bm{:}D_{it}$ \\
E2 (G3) & Exposure-weighted & $X = D_{bc,it}$ & $bm{:}D_{bc,it}$ \\
E7 (G4) & AIC-selected $\hat t_{1/2}$ & E2 form, $\hat t_{1/2}$ chosen by AIC over $\{0.25,0.5,1.0,2.0\}$ wk & $bm{:}D_{bc,it}$ \\
E9 (G5) & Paired-difference & two-stage OLS on per-patient on/off means & biomarker slope \\
E1cr2, E3cr2, E2cr2, E7cr2 (G6--G9) & CR2 variants & E1/E3/E2/E7 formula, CR2 cluster-robust SE & (as above) \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\textbf{E3, the lag-adjusted (Jones--Kenward) specification.} The
nuisance indicator $L_{it} = D_{i,t-1}(1-D_{it})$ marks off-drug
timepoints that immediately follow an on-drug one; it enters as a main
effect only, so the interaction actually tested remains
$bm{:}D_{it}$, not $bm{:}L_{it}$.

\textbf{E7, the AIC-selected half-life.} With $M(t_{1/2})$ the E2 fit
at a candidate half-life,
\begin{equation}
\hat t_{1/2} = \operatorname*{arg\,min}_{t_{1/2}\in\mathcal T}
  \mathrm{AIC}\big(M(t_{1/2})\big), \qquad \mathrm{AIC}(M) =
  -2\log L(M) + 2p, \qquad \mathcal T = \{0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0\},
\label{eq:e7-aic}
\end{equation}
with the reported coefficient, SE, and $p$-value read from
$M(\hat t_{1/2})$ alone. Paper 02 notes the post-selection-inference
risk explicitly (Berk et al., 2013): treating $\hat t_{1/2}$ as fixed
in advance understates the sampling variability of the reported
interaction estimate.

\textbf{E9, the paired-difference specification.} A correlation-
structure-agnostic two-stage estimator. For patient $i$ with
$n_i^{\mathrm{on}}, n_i^{\mathrm{off}} > 0$,
\begin{equation}
\bar Y_{i}^{\mathrm{on}} = \frac{1}{n_i^{\mathrm{on}}}
  \sum_{t:\,D_{it}=1} Y_{it}, \qquad
\bar Y_{i}^{\mathrm{off}} = \frac{1}{n_i^{\mathrm{off}}}
  \sum_{t:\,D_{it}=0} Y_{it}, \qquad
\Delta_i = \bar Y_{i}^{\mathrm{on}} - \bar Y_{i}^{\mathrm{off}},
\label{eq:e9-diffs}
\end{equation}
followed by a second-stage OLS regression on the biomarker,
\begin{equation}
\Delta_i = \beta_0 + \beta_{bm:D}^{\mathrm{E9}}\, B_i + e_i, \qquad i =
  1,\dots,N',
\label{eq:e9-ols}
\end{equation}
with the interaction test on $\beta_{bm:D}^{\mathrm{E9}}$ and
$N' \le N$ patients contributing (those without both on- and off-drug
observations are dropped). Paper 01 (as restated) derives the moments
and power of this statistic in closed form: its variance does not
depend on $t_{1/2}$, and its population slope is
$-c_{bm}(\sigma_{BR}/\sigma_{bm})(1 - \bar g_{\mathrm{off}})$, with
$\bar g_{\mathrm{off}}$ the mean coupling over a path's post-baseline
off-drug visits (Paper 01, Propositions 1 and 2, Appendix A.1).

\note{2026-10-08: notation. $S_{x,it}$ and $bm_i$ were replaced by the
canonical $Y_{it}$ and $B_i$ (\code{NOTATION.md} Rule 3), and the
second-stage slope and residual, formerly $\beta_1$ and
$\varepsilon_i$, were renamed so they do not collide with the
treatment effect (Paper 07's $\beta_1$, now $\beta_D$) of \S\ref{sec:architectures} and the
observation-level residual $\varepsilon_{it}$.}

An earlier, retired pair of specifications, E5 (adding
$bm{:}L_{it}$) and E6 (adding $bm{:}t_{sd}$ as a direct
carryover-by-biomarker interaction), underperformed the Unadjusted
specification at every cell tested and were replaced by E7/E9; they
are recorded here as historical context, not part of the active
nine-specification set.

\subsection{Analysis strategies outside the E1--E9 set}
\label{sec:inva-altanalysis}

The pre-restatement Paper~01 (Section~6, as summarized in
\code{docs/23}, Section~10) listed analysis strategies for recovering
power under covariance moderation that are not among the nine
specifications of Table~\ref{tab:especs}. Two carry a model statement.

\textbf{Weighted analysis.} Each observation receives weight 1 on drug
and $e^{-\lambda t_{sd,it}}$ off drug, that is, the weight
$D_{bc,it}$ of Equation~\eqref{eq:dbc}, on the argument that off-drug
visits carry less coupling (argued).

\textbf{Two-stage random slopes.} The first stage fits a random drug
slope without the biomarker,
\begin{equation}
Y_{it} = \beta_0 + \beta_T t + \beta_D D_{it} + u_{0i} + u_{1i}D_{it}
  + \varepsilon_{it},
\label{eq:inva-twostage-1}
\end{equation}
and the second regresses the predicted drug slopes on the biomarker,
\begin{equation}
\hat u_{1i} = \xi_0 + \xi_1 B_i + e_i,
\label{eq:inva-twostage-2}
\end{equation}
testing $\xi_1 = 0$. (Source: \code{docs/23}, Section~10, item 6.4.)
\note{Not evaluated in any source. Because the biomarker is omitted
from the first stage, $\hat u_{1i}$ is a best linear unbiased predictor
shrunk toward zero by a participant-specific reliability factor, so
$\hat\xi_1$ estimates the interaction attenuated by that factor; the
second-stage test also ignores the prediction error of $\hat u_{1i}$
(argued; standard properties of predicted random effects, not checked
for these designs). \code{docs/23} renamed the source's $\delta_0$,
$\delta_1$, $\eta_i$ to $\xi_0$, $\xi_1$, $e_i$; the first-stage time
slope, written $\beta_1$ in the source, is $\beta_T$ here as in
\S\ref{sec:analysis}.}

The within-subject contrast of the same list is E9
(Equation~\eqref{eq:e9-ols}). A third proposal, a linear
$t_{sd}$ main-effect covariate added to the binary specification
(\code{Sx \textasciitilde\ bm + t + Db + bm:Db + tsd}, ``Approach B''
of \code{docs/23}, Section~12.2), is the package option
\code{op\$simplecarryover} (\code{R/lme\_analysis.R}, inspected); it is
neither E3 nor any member of Table~\ref{tab:especs}, and no stored
result for it was located.

\subsection{Carryover bias of the classical two-period crossover
estimator}
\label{sec:inva-crossover}

For an AB/BA crossover with period effects $\mu_{(1)}$, $\mu_{(2)}$,
period-specific treatment effects $\beta_D^{(1)}$, $\beta_D^{(2)}$
(active minus control), and additive carryover $\chi_1$ into the second
period after active treatment and $\chi_0$ after control, the expected
cell means are
$\mu_{(1)} + \upsilon_{A,1}$, $\mu_{(2)} + \upsilon_{B,2} + \chi_1$ (sequence AB)
and $\mu_{(1)} + \upsilon_{B,1}$, $\mu_{(2)} + \upsilon_{A,2} + \chi_0$
(sequence BA). The basic estimator
$\hat\beta_D = \tfrac12[(\bar Y_{AB,1} - \bar Y_{AB,2}) -
(\bar Y_{BA,1} - \bar Y_{BA,2})]$ then has expectation
\begin{equation}
E(\hat\beta_D) = \tfrac12\big(\beta_D^{(1)} + \beta_D^{(2)}\big)
  - \tfrac12\big(\chi_1 - \chi_0\big)
\label{eq:inva-crossover}
\end{equation}
(derived; checked numerically, \code{check-historical-results.R}, block B). The
period effects cancel, and the estimator is biased by half the
difference of the two carryover effects; with no carryover from control
($\chi_0 = 0$) the bias is $-\chi_1/2$, toward zero when the carryover
has the sign of the treatment effect. (Source: \code{docs/09a},
Section~2.3, attributing the result to Shi and Ye, 2024.)
\note{\code{docs/09a} writes $E(\hat\tau) = \tfrac12(\tau_1 +
\tilde\tau_2 - \omega_0 - \omega_1)$, with the carryover terms summed.
Under the additive model above the two carryovers enter with opposite
signs; the source's form agrees only if $\omega_0$ is defined with the
opposite sign (for example, as an effect on the control contrast), and
both forms agree when $\chi_0 = 0$. The Shi and Ye (2024) full text was
not available in PubMed Central (only the abstract, which states the
direction of the bias under a sign condition), so their convention was
not checked. Symbols renamed: $\tau_1$, $\tilde\tau_2$, $\omega_0$,
$\omega_1$ of the source are $\beta_D^{(1)}$, $\beta_D^{(2)}$, $\chi_0$,
$\chi_1$ here; the cell-level $\upsilon_{A,1}$, \dots, $\upsilon_{B,2}$ (treatment, period) are local
and are not the contrast variance $\sigma_\Delta^2$ of \S\ref{sec:cg-moments}.
2026-10-09: notation. The cell effects were written $\tau_{A,1}$,
\dots; $\tau^2$ is the canonical heterogeneity variance.}

\subsection{A constant-exposure analysis under carryover: the
historical OL+BDC study}
\label{sec:inva-09a}

\code{docs/09a} (a pmsimstats2025-era study, reviewed 2026-10-07)
simulates a single-path design: on drug at weeks 1--8, off drug from
week 9, visits at weeks 1--12, 16 and 20, $N = 70$. Its
data-generating process is mean moderation with linear growth,
\begin{equation}
BR_{it} = \begin{cases}
  a_{BR}\, t_{od,it}\,(1 + \beta_{bm} b_i) & \text{on drug}, \\
  a_{BR}\, t_{od}^{\mathrm{disc}}\,(1 + \beta_{bm} b_i)\,
    2^{-t_{sd,it}/t_{1/2}} & \text{off drug},\ t_{1/2} > 0, \\
  0 & \text{off drug},\ t_{1/2} = 0,
\end{cases}
\qquad Y_{it} = 50 - BR_{it} + \varepsilon_{it},
\label{eq:inva-09a-dgp}
\end{equation}
with $a_{BR} = 0.5$ per week, $\beta_{bm} = 0.3$,
$t_{od}^{\mathrm{disc}} = 8$, $\varepsilon_{it} \sim \mathcal N(0, 2^2)$
independent, no random intercept and no serial correlation. The
analysis regresses $Y$ on $b_i$, $t_{od}$ and $b_i t_{od}$ with a random
intercept, where $t_{od}$ stays at 8 after discontinuation. On drug,
$E(Y \mid b_i) = 50 - a_{BR} t_{od} - a_{BR}\beta_{bm}\,b_i t_{od}$, so
the target is
\begin{equation}
\beta_{bm:D} = -a_{BR}\,\beta_{bm} = -0.15
\label{eq:inva-09a-target}
\end{equation}
per week on drug and per standard deviation of the biomarker (derived;
Source: \code{docs/09a}, Section~3.3). \note{The source writes $a$ for
$a_{BR}$; $a_t$ is used in \S\ref{sec:cg-moments} for contrast
weights. $t_{sd}$ is counted from the first off-drug visit (week 9,
$t_{sd} = 0$), unlike the package, where the first off-drug visit has
$t_{sd} \ge 1$. Under this convention the week-9 response is full at
every $t_{1/2} > 0$ and zero at $t_{1/2} = 0$, a discontinuity at
$t_{1/2} = 0$ of the same kind as the step rule's
(\S\ref{sec:cg-step}); whether it accounts for part of the simulated
change between $t_{1/2} = 0$ and $0.05$ was not tested.}

\textbf{Retention.} The off-drug retention $2^{-t_{sd}/t_{1/2}}$ at the
measured off-drug weeks is given in Table~\ref{tab:inva-retention}
(verified, \code{check-historical-results.R}, block A).

\begin{table}[h]
\centering\small
\caption{Off-drug retention $2^{-t_{sd}/t_{1/2}}$ in the
\code{docs/09a} design (Source: \code{docs/09a}, Section~5.4;
verified).}
\label{tab:inva-retention}
\begin{tabular}{@{}ccccccc@{}}
\toprule
Week & $t_{sd}$ & $t_{1/2} = 0.1$ & 0.2 & 0.5 & 1.0 & 2.0 \\
\midrule
9  & 0  & 1.000 & 1.000 & 1.000 & 1.000 & 1.000 \\
10 & 1  & 0.001 & 0.031 & 0.250 & 0.500 & 0.707 \\
12 & 3  & 0.000 & 0.000 & 0.016 & 0.125 & 0.354 \\
16 & 7  & 0.000 & 0.000 & 0.000 & 0.008 & 0.088 \\
20 & 11 & 0.000 & 0.000 & 0.000 & 0.000 & 0.022 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\textbf{The misspecification integral.} Off drug the true response is
$Q_i\,2^{-s/t_{1/2}}$, $Q_i = a_{BR}t_{od}^{\mathrm{disc}}(1 +
\beta_{bm}b_i)$ the response at discontinuation and $s$ the time since
discontinuation, while a predictor constant off drug predicts a
constant $\mu^\ast$. Over an off-drug span of length $T_{\mathrm{off}}$,
with $k = \ln 2/t_{1/2}$,
\begin{equation}
\mathcal V(t_{1/2}) = \int_0^{T_{\mathrm{off}}}
  \big[Q_i\,2^{-s/t_{1/2}} - \mu^\ast\big]^2\,ds .
\label{eq:inva-V}
\end{equation}
Two choices of $\mu^\ast$ give opposite conclusions (derived; closed
forms checked against numerical quadrature, \code{check-historical-results.R},
block \code{A2}):
\begin{itemize}[itemsep=2pt]
\item $\mu^\ast = Q_i$, the on-drug level implied by a constant
  $t_{od}$ with a common slope:
  \begin{equation}
  \mathcal V = Q_i^2\Big[T_{\mathrm{off}} - \frac{2(1 - e^{-kT_{\mathrm{off}}})}{k}
    + \frac{1 - e^{-2kT_{\mathrm{off}}}}{2k}\Big],
  \label{eq:inva-V-fixed}
  \end{equation}
  which falls strictly and monotonically from $Q_i^2T_{\mathrm{off}}$
  as $t_{1/2}\to0$ to 0 as $t_{1/2}\to\infty$, since the integrand
  falls pointwise in $t_{1/2}$. There is no interior maximum, and
  $t_{1/2} = 0$ is the case of largest mismatch.
\item $\mu^\ast$ the least-squares constant over the span:
  \begin{equation}
  \mathcal V = Q_i^2\Big[\frac{1 - e^{-2kT_{\mathrm{off}}}}{2k}
    - \frac{(1 - e^{-kT_{\mathrm{off}}})^2}{k^2T_{\mathrm{off}}}\Big]
    = T_{\mathrm{off}}\,\mathrm{Var}_s\big(Q_i 2^{-s/t_{1/2}}\big),
  \label{eq:inva-V-ls}
  \end{equation}
  which is 0 in both limits and positive between them. Since
  $\mathcal V/(Q_i^2T_{\mathrm{off}})$ depends on $t_{1/2}$ only through
  $t_{1/2}/T_{\mathrm{off}}$, its maximum is at
  $t_{1/2} = 0.214\,T_{\mathrm{off}}$, where it equals 0.066 (verified,
  numerical optimization).
\end{itemize}
(Source: \code{docs/09a}, Section~3.5, which states the two cases and
the monotone limits; the closed forms and the location of the maximum
are derived here.) The fitted model is neither case exactly, since one
interaction slope serves both phases; the simulated attenuation of
\code{docs/09a}, Section~5.1 (largest at $t_{1/2} = 0$, where the mean
estimate changes sign, and shrinking with $t_{1/2}$) behaves like the
first case, so the source's bias-variance account of a power minimum
at an intermediate half-life is not supported (\code{docs/09a}, review
of 2026-10-07; argued). \note{\code{docs/09a} originally claimed an
interior maximum without qualification and gave the $t_{1/2}\to0$ limit
as $\mu^{\ast2}$; both were corrected in the source. Its simulated
power (0.06 at $t_{1/2} = 0$, 0.00--0.02 at 0.05--0.5 weeks, 0.36 at
2 weeks; 100 replicates, MCSE at most 0.05) is quoted, not recomputed.
The published Hendrickson collapse is explained by the step coupling
rule (\S\ref{sec:cg-step}), which a mean-moderation model of this kind
cannot exhibit.}

%=============================================================
\section{Baseline, a prognostic biomarker, and the shared biomarker
effect}
\label{sec:bp-prog}
%=============================================================

The published Hybrid analysis treats baseline as a ninth response row,
coded off drug at $t = 0$. This section collects what that implies for
the interaction (Sources: \code{docs/41}, \code{docs/45},
\code{docs/48}, \code{docs/49}; Paper 01, Appendix A.4). Local symbols:
$\beta_B$, the analysis model's biomarker main effect (not the
canonical multiplier $\beta_{bm}$); $\beta_{BL,t}$, the slope of visit $t$ on
baseline, and $\boldsymbol\beta_{BL}$ the vector of these slopes
\note{2026-10-09: notation. Written $\phi_t$, $\boldsymbol\phi$ and
$\phi_{\mathrm{BL}}$ before; $\phi$ is reserved for the decay
families}; $s_0$, $s_t$, the true slopes of $Y$ on the standardized
biomarker $b_i$ at baseline and at visit $t$; $\eta_t$, the expectancy
(0 at baseline, 1 open label, 0.5 blinded); $n_{\text{on}}$,
$n_{\text{off}}$, a path's post-baseline on- and off-drug visits.
The prognostic pathways and the slopes they imply are listed in
\S\ref{sec:bp-pathways}, and the separation of the biomarker block, on
which the leak computations rest, is \S\ref{sec:bp-sep}.

\subsection{Two codings of baseline and five propositions}

Write $\mathbf{Y}_i = (Y_{i0}, \mathbf{Y}_{ip}')'$, with
$E(Y_{i0}) = \mu_0 + \beta_B B_i$, $E(\mathbf{Y}_{ip}) =
X_{ip}\boldsymbol\beta$, and partitioned covariance with blocks
$\sigma_{00}$, $\boldsymbol\sigma_{p0}$ and $\Sigma_{pp}$. Baseline is
off drug, so neither $\beta_D$ nor $\beta_{bm:D}$ enters its mean. The
\emph{covariate coding} fits only the post-baseline visits,
\begin{equation}
Y_{it} = \beta_0 + \beta_{BL}\,\mathrm{BL}_i + \beta_B B_i
  + \beta_D D_{it} + f(t) + \beta_{bm:D}\,B_i D_{it} + \varepsilon_{it},
  \qquad t = 1, \dots, 8 .
\label{eq:bp-ancova}
\end{equation}
Assume multivariate normal outcomes, a correct mean model and complete
data. (Source: \code{docs/41}, Sections 2 and 4.)

\textbf{Proposition 1 (the covariate model is the conditional model).}
\begin{equation}
\mathbf{Y}_{ip} \mid Y_{i0} \sim \mathcal N\!\Big(X_{ip}\boldsymbol\beta
  + \boldsymbol\beta_{BL}\,(Y_{i0} - \mu_0 - \beta_B B_i),\;
  \Sigma_{pp} - \boldsymbol\sigma_{p0}\boldsymbol\sigma_{p0}'/\sigma_{00}
  \Big), \qquad \boldsymbol\beta_{BL} = \boldsymbol\sigma_{p0}/\sigma_{00}.
\label{eq:bp-prop1}
\end{equation}
Conditioning changes the intercept and the biomarker main effect but
not $\beta_D$ or $\beta_{bm:D}$, so the interaction estimand is the
same under both codings (derived). In the published process
$Y_{it} = \mathrm{BL}_i - S_{it}$ with $\mathrm{BL}_i$ independent of
every response component and of the biomarker, so
$\sigma_{t0} = \sigma_{00}$ and $\boldsymbol\beta_{BL} = \mathbf 1$: the
covariate analysis is a change-from-baseline analysis (verified in the
source to six decimals, \code{02-baseline-propositions.R}).

\textbf{Proposition 2 (no gain for a within-participant contrast with
equal slopes).} For weights $\mathbf a$ on the post-baseline visits
with $\sum_t a_t = 0$,
\begin{equation}
\operatorname{Var}(\mathbf a'\mathbf Y_{ip} \mid Y_{i0})
  = \mathbf a'\Sigma_{pp}\mathbf a - \sigma_{00}(\mathbf a'\boldsymbol\beta_{BL})^2 ,
\label{eq:bp-prop2}
\end{equation}
and the reduction vanishes whenever $\beta_{BL,t} \equiv \bar\beta_{BL}$ (derived).
Measurement error in baseline attenuates every slope by the same
reliability factor and so does not create a gain; a gain requires
baseline to predict on-drug and off-drug visits differently, for
example severity-moderated response or a time-varying natural course
confounded with drug state. In the construct the E9 contrast variance
is 44.13 (paths 1, 2) and 45.03 (paths 3, 4) with or without
conditioning. \note{Recomputed here from the compound-symmetry moment
formula of Paper 01, Appendix A.1, as 44.13 and 45.02
(\code{check-design.R}); the last digit differs by rounding.}

\textbf{Proposition 3 (between participants).}
$\operatorname{Var}(Y_{it} - wY_{i0})$ is minimized at $w = \beta_{BL,t}$,
with minimum $\sigma_{tt} - \sigma_{t0}^2/\sigma_{00}$ (derived): for a
blinded visit, 599 against 257, a gain for $\beta_B$ and path means,
not for the interaction.

\textbf{Proposition 4 (the baseline row as an off-drug visit).} The
response coding's paired-difference analogue, E9b, includes baseline in
the off-drug mean. Substituting $Y_{i0} = \mathrm{BL}_i$,
\begin{equation}
\Delta_i^{\mathrm{E9}} = -\bar S_{i,\text{on}} + \bar S_{i,\text{off}},
\qquad
\Delta_i^{\mathrm{E9b}} = -\bar S_{i,\text{on}}
  + \frac{n_{\text{off}}}{n_{\text{off}}+1}\,\bar S_{i,\text{off}} .
\label{eq:bp-e9b}
\end{equation}
With $g_t$ the coupling weight (1 on drug, $e^{-\lambda t_{sd}}$ off
drug after exposure), the E9b slope is
$-c_{bm}(\sigma_{BR}/\sigma_{bm})\big(\bar g_{\text{on}} -
\tfrac{n_{\text{off}}}{n_{\text{off}}+1}\bar g_{\text{off}}\big)$.
Under the published step rule with any carryover,
$\bar g_{\text{on}} = \bar g_{\text{off}} = 1$, so the E9 slope is zero
and the E9b slope is $1/(n_{\text{off}}+1)$ of the no-carryover value:
the power floor of the published analysis. Writing
$S_{it} = s_i + d_{it}$ with shared level variance $\sigma_s^2$, E9b
retains $-s_i/(n_{\text{off}}+1)$ and so adds
$\sigma_s^2/(n_{\text{off}}+1)^2$ to the contrast variance before the
partial offset from the shrunken visit-specific terms (derived). The
source reports 44.13 to 51.04 and 45.03 to 51.77 (verified there).
\note{The two algebraic identities were re-derived; the exact split of
the increase, and the totals, are recomputed in
Equation~\eqref{eq:invf-e9b-exact} of \S\ref{sec:invf-blcov}.}

\textbf{Proposition 5 (working covariance).} The GLS estimator under
working $V$ has variance $A^{-1}MA^{-1}$, $A = \sum_i X_i'V^{-1}X_i$,
$M = \sum_i X_i'V^{-1}\Sigma V^{-1}X_i$, and the model-based $A^{-1}$
is correct when $V \propto \Sigma$. The baseline row has no noise of
its own ($\sigma_{00} = \sigma_{t0}$), so no random-intercept $V$ over
nine rows is proportional to $\Sigma$ (derived).

\textbf{cLDA and ANCOVA.} With complete data and an unstructured
covariance the likelihood factorizes as $f(Y_{i0})f(\mathbf
Y_{ip}\mid Y_{i0})$, and the row and covariate models coincide
\emph{provided} $\beta_B$ is free at baseline (derived). The published
row coding shares $\beta_B$ between baseline and the post-baseline
off-drug visits and does not coincide. \note{\code{docs/41}, Section 7,
applied the equivalence to the shared row coding; \code{docs/45},
Section 6, corrected this, verified by its \code{us-KR-sepbm} arm.}

\subsection{The construct's outcome covariance and the exact cost of the
baseline row}
\label{sec:invf-blcov}

\textbf{The covariance.} In the published process
$Y_{it} = \mathrm{BL}_i - S_{it}$ for $t \ge 1$, with $\mathrm{BL}_i$
independent of the biomarker and of every response component, so
\begin{equation}
\sigma_{t0} = \sigma_{00} \ \text{for every } t \ge 1, \qquad
\Sigma_{pp} = \sigma_{00}\,J + \operatorname{Cov}(\mathbf S_{ip}),
\label{eq:invf-sigmapp}
\end{equation}
with $J$ the matrix of ones (derived; Source: \code{docs/41}, Section 3).
With $\sigma_{BL} = 18.48$, $\sigma_{TV} = 10$, $\sigma_{PB,t} =
10\eta_t$, $\sigma_{BR} = 8$, $\rho = 0.8$, $c_1 = 0.2$ and $c_\times =
0.1$, $\operatorname{Var}(S_t) = \sigma_{TV}^2 + \sigma_{PB,t}^2 +
\sigma_{BR}^2 + 2c_1(\sigma_{TV}\sigma_{PB,t} + \sigma_{TV}\sigma_{BR} +
\sigma_{PB,t}\sigma_{BR})$ is 368.0 at an open-label visit and 257.0 at a
blinded one; with $\sigma_{00} = \sigma_{BL}^2 = 341.6$ these give the
unconditional and conditional entries of
Table~\ref{tab:invc-outcome-cov} (path 1, no interaction; verified,
\code{check-baseline-design-search.R}). Baseline has no noise of its own (its
covariance with every later visit equals its variance), so its
correlation with later visits, 0.694 (open label) and 0.755 (blinded),
is lower than theirs with each other, 0.852 and 0.880 (derived).

\textbf{Proposition 3 in full.} For a single visit,
\begin{equation}
\operatorname{Var}(Y_{it}) = \sigma_{tt}, \qquad
\operatorname{Var}(Y_{it} - Y_{i0}) = \sigma_{tt} + \sigma_{00}
  - 2\sigma_{t0}, \qquad
\operatorname{Var}(Y_{it} \mid Y_{i0}) = \sigma_{tt}
  - \sigma_{t0}^2/\sigma_{00},
\label{eq:invf-prop3}
\end{equation}
the cases $w = 0$, $w = 1$ and $w = \beta_{BL,t}$ of
$\operatorname{Var}(Y_{it} - wY_{i0})$. The conditional variance is never
the largest, and it equals the change-score variance exactly when
$\beta_{BL,t} = 1$ (derived). At a blinded visit the three are 598.6, 257.0 and
257.0 (verified).

\textbf{The residual factor for the interaction.} Adding baseline to the
E9 regression of $\Delta_i = \mathbf a'\mathbf Y_{ip}$ on $B_i$ multiplies
the residual variance by $1 - r^2_{\Delta,\mathrm{BL}}$, with
\begin{equation}
r^2_{\Delta,\mathrm{BL}} = \frac{(\mathbf a'\boldsymbol\sigma_{p0})^2}
  {\sigma_{00}\,\mathbf a'\Sigma_{pp}\mathbf a}
\label{eq:invf-r2}
\end{equation}
and costs one degree of freedom (derived; Source: \code{docs/41},
Proposition 2; the source renamed $\rho^2$ to $r^2_{\Delta,\mathrm{BL}}$).
By Equation~\eqref{eq:invf-sigmapp}, $\mathbf a'\boldsymbol\sigma_{p0} =
\sigma_{00}\sum_t a_t = 0$ for every contrast, so the factor is exactly 1
in the construct.

\textbf{Proposition 4, exactly.} Since $\Delta_i^{\mathrm{E9b}} =
\Delta_i^{\mathrm{E9}} - \bar S_{i,\text{off}}/(n_{\text{off}}+1)$
(Equation~\eqref{eq:bp-e9b}),
\begin{equation}
\operatorname{Var}(\Delta^{\mathrm{E9b}}) = \operatorname{Var}(\Delta^{\mathrm{E9}})
  + \frac{\operatorname{Var}(\bar S_{\text{off}})}{(n_{\text{off}}+1)^2}
  - \frac{2\operatorname{Cov}(\Delta^{\mathrm{E9}}, \bar S_{\text{off}})}
         {n_{\text{off}}+1}
\label{eq:invf-e9b-exact}
\end{equation}
(derived). The second term is the shared response level that E9b retains;
the third is the offset from the shrunken off-drug mean. On paths 1 and 2
($n_{\text{off}} = 3$), $\operatorname{Var}(\bar S_{\text{off}}) =
209.13$ and $\operatorname{Cov} = 12.33$, so the addition is 13.07 and
the offset 6.17 (47\%), giving $44.13 \to 51.04$; on paths 3 and 4
($n_{\text{off}} = 4$), 203.15 and 3.45, an addition of 8.13 and an
offset of 1.38 (17\%), giving $45.03 \to 51.77$ (verified). \note{\code{docs/41},
Proposition 4(b), approximates the addition by $\sigma_s^2/(n_{\text{off}}
+ 1)^2$ with $\sigma_s^2 \approx 200$, that is 12.5 and 8.0, and the
offsets by 45\% and 16\% of those; the exact split of
Equation~\eqref{eq:invf-e9b-exact} is 13.07 and 8.13, 47\% and 17\%. The
totals agree.}

The closed-form E9 and E9b powers that follow are those of
Table~\ref{tab:cg-power} and, for mean moderation under E9b, of
Table~\ref{tab:inve-slope}. The standard deviations of the pooled
slopes, $\mathrm{SD} = \sigma_{\mathrm{res}}/(\sigma_{bm}\sqrt{N-3})$
with $\sigma_{\mathrm{res}}$ of Equation~\eqref{eq:cg-pooled}, are
0.0507 (E9) and 0.0549 (E9b) under the step rule at $t_{1/2} = 0$,
0.0531 and 0.0571 at every positive half-life, and 0.0531 and 0.0572
(0.0571 at $t_{1/2} = 1$) under mean moderation (Hybrid, CS, $N = 70$,
strength 0.25; Source: \code{docs/41}, Section 6, from \code{docs/37};
verified, \code{check-baseline-design-search.R}; the E9 values agree with
Table~\ref{tab:inve-mc-hybrid}). Under the step rule the SD rises from
0.0507 to 0.0531 because covariance binding stops explaining variance
once the slope vanishes (Equation~\eqref{eq:cg-tau}); under mean
moderation it is 0.0531 throughout. \note{The source gives one
mean-moderation row for $t_{1/2} = 0$ to 1 (E9b power 0.619); the E9b
power moves to 0.620 at $t_{1/2} = 1$ through the path-mean term of
Equation~\eqref{eq:cg-pooled}.}

\subsection{The leak through a shared biomarker effect}

The probability limit of the biomarker block is
Equation~\eqref{eq:bp-plim} (\S\ref{sec:bp-sep}).

\textbf{The leak, and which factor is correct.} For S under a
natural-course pathway with no interaction,
$\mathbf s = s_{\text{prog}}(0, 1, \dots, 1)'$. Under working
independence with binary $D$, the fit reproduces the on-drug rows
exactly and gives $\hat\beta_B$ the weighted mean of the baseline and
post-baseline off-drug slopes. With $n_0$ and $n_3$ the row counts
of the baseline rows and of the post-baseline off-drug rows
(\note{2026-10-09: notation. Written $w_0$, $w_3$ before; $w_t$ is the
calendar week, and these weights are row counts.}),
\begin{equation}
\hat\beta_B \to f\,s_{\text{prog}}, \qquad
\hat\beta_{bm:D} \to (1 - f)\,s_{\text{prog}}, \qquad
f = \frac{n_3}{n_0 + n_3}, \quad 1 - f = \frac{n_0}{n_0 + n_3}
\label{eq:bp-leak}
\end{equation}
(derived). So with $f$ defined as the share of $s_{\text{prog}}$ that
the shared $\beta_B$ absorbs, as in \code{docs/48}, \code{docs/49} and
Paper 01, Appendix A.4, the spurious interaction is
$(1 - f)\,s_{\text{prog}}$, and $1 - f$ is the effective share of the
baseline row. Per path with equal visit weights,
$1 - f = 1/(n_{\text{off}} + 1)$, the same factor as Proposition 4.
Pooled over the Hybrid paths at the published allocation (18, 18, 17,
17), $n_0 = 70$ and $n_3 = 244$, so $1 - f = 0.223$ (verified,
\code{check-prognostic.R}). The leak is a fixed bias, so its rejection
rate grows with $N$. Under a correlated working covariance the bias is
still proportional to $s_{\text{prog}}$, but its factor is the second
element of \eqref{eq:bp-plim}, not a simple weight share, and it can
be larger than under independence.

Computed factors $\operatorname{plim}\hat\beta_{bm:D}/s_{\text{prog}}$
in the \code{04-power-simulation.R} construct, the published construct
in the CS row and the \code{04-power-simulation.R} construct with AR(1)
components in the AR(1) rows (Hybrid, $N = 70$; $D_{bc}$ at an assumed
$t_{1/2}^a = 0.5$ week, pre-exposure coded 0; verified,
\code{check-prognostic.R} and \code{check-prognostic-car1.R}):

\begin{table}[h]
\centering\small
\caption{Spurious interaction per unit prognostic slope. ``True'' uses
the construct's own outcome covariance as $V$ (an approximation to an
unstructured fit); RI+CAR1 uses the covariance to which a large ML fit
converged ($\sigma_u^2 = 356$, $\sigma_\varepsilon^2 = 248$, CAR(1) parameter
$\hat\rho_a = 0.64$ per week).}
\label{tab:bp-leak}
\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrr@{}}
\toprule
Construct, working $V$ & Pathway & S & F1 & ES \\
\midrule
any, independence & natural course, constant & 0.271 & $-0.020$ & 0 \\
any, independence & placebo, $\propto\eta_t$ & 0.369 & 0 & 0.239 \\
CS, true & natural course, constant & 0.091 & $-0.013$ & 0 \\
AR(1) $\rho = 0.8$, true & natural course, constant & 0.430 & 0.171 & 0 \\
AR(1) $\rho = 0.7$, RI+CAR1 & natural course, constant & 0.276 & 0.089 & 0 \\
AR(1) $\rho = 0.7$, RI+CAR1 & placebo, $\propto\eta_t$ & 0.311 & 0 & 0.139 \\

\noindent The ordering of S across working covariances matches the
stress-test rejection rates of \code{docs/48}, Section 2 (S 0.099,
unstructured 0.416), which supports, but does not prove, the weighting
account that the source labels inferred. \note{These factors are
computed in the \code{04-power-simulation.R} construct (with AR(1)
components in the AR(1) rows; 2026-10-09: previously called the
published process, which under \code{NOTATION.md} is compound symmetry
only), not in the stress-test construct
(configuration C, $\rho = 0.7$; Table~\ref{tab:invd-leakC});
they illustrate the mechanism and are not a reproduction of the
stress-test rates.}

\subsection{Freeing the baseline slope, and the expectancy terms}

\textbf{ES} adds $B_i\cdot\mathbf 1\{t = 0\}$, giving baseline its own
slope $\beta_B + \beta_{B,0}$. For any pathway constant after baseline,
$\mathbf s = s_{\text{prog}}(\mathbf 1 - \mathbf 1\{t = 0\})$ lies in
the span of $(1, D_{bc}, \mathbf 1\{t=0\})$, so by \eqref{eq:bp-plim}
the leak is exactly zero under every working covariance (derived;
Table~\ref{tab:bp-leak}). ES is equivalent to the covariate coding
(\code{docs/45}, Section 6).

\textbf{F1} adds $\eta_t + B_i\eta_t$, with coefficient $\beta_{bm:\eta}$
on $B_i\eta_t$. Because $\eta_0 = 0$, the baseline slope is freed
implicitly, and an expectancy-scaled pathway
$\mathbf s \propto (\eta_0, \dots, \eta_8)'$ lies in the span, so F1 is
exact for it under every working covariance (derived). For a pathway
constant after baseline it is not in the span. With binary $D$ and
working independence, group the rows into baseline, open label on
drug, blinded on drug and blinded off drug, with row counts $n_0$,
$n_1$, $n_2$, $n_3$. The fitted slopes $(\beta_B,\ \beta_B +
\beta_{bm:\eta} + \beta_{bm:D},\ \beta_B + \tfrac12\beta_{bm:\eta} +
\beta_{bm:D},\ \beta_B + \tfrac12\beta_{bm:\eta})$ satisfy the single
linear restriction $\hat s_0 + \hat s_1 = \hat s_2 + \hat s_3$, and the weighted projection
of $s_{\text{prog}}(0,1,1,1)$ onto it gives
\begin{equation}
\hat\beta_{bm:D} \to s_{\text{prog}}\,
  \frac{1/n_3 - 1/n_2}{1/n_0 + 1/n_1 + 1/n_2 + 1/n_3}
\label{eq:bp-f1-leak}
\end{equation}
(derived here). The misfit therefore cancels exactly if and only if the
blinded on-drug and blinded off-drug visits carry equal total weight,
$n_2 = n_3$, whatever the weights of baseline and open label; this is
the condition stated in \code{docs/49}, Section 3. In the Hybrid design
at the published allocation $n_2 = 176$ and $n_3 = 244$, so the
condition fails and the factor is $-0.051$ (binary $D$; verified,
\code{check-prognostic.R}). \note{\code{docs/49} says a residual bias
``can remain'' with unequal weights or serial correlation; the
computation shows it does remain in the Hybrid design, small under
independence ($-0.020$ with $D_{bc}$) but $0.089$ under the RI+CAR1
working covariance and $0.171$ under the true AR(1) covariance. The
simulated F1 rejection of 0.047 in the constant-pathway cell is
consistent with a bias of this size, but F1's robustness there is
approximate, not exact.} Conversely, ES is not exact for the
expectancy-scaled pathway (factor 0.139 to 0.239), although its
simulated rejection rate was nominal (0.049). \note{\code{docs/48},
Section 3, states that under ES ``nothing that happens at baseline can
leak''; that is correct for baseline, but an expectancy-scaled slope
still leaks into ES through the open-label visits.} In F1 the
interaction rests mainly on the blinded on-drug versus blinded
off-drug comparison, at equal expectancy.

\subsection{The power cost of freeing the baseline slope}

Size-adjusted power, each analysis rejecting at its own null 5\%
quantile (Source: \code{docs/48}, Sections 1 and 3; \code{docs/49},
Section 4; verified there from the stress-test outputs):

\begin{table}[h]
\centering\small
\caption{Size-adjusted power of the shared (S) and freed (ES, F1)
analyses.}
\label{tab:bp-cost}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lrrr@{}}
\toprule
Cell & S & ES & F1 \\
\midrule
AR(1), $c_{bm} = 0.30$ & 0.514 & 0.364 & 0.364 \\
AR(1), $c_{bm} = 0.45$ & 0.808 & 0.656 & 0.672 \\
CS, $c_{bm} = 0.25$ & 0.554 & 0.662 & 0.522 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\noindent Under AR(1) data the baseline row is an informative off-drug
observation of the biomarker slope that the participant effect does
not replace, and freeing it costs about 30\% of the power; an analytic
account of this gain has not been derived (\code{docs/45}, Section 12,
question 4). \note{The sources summarize the CS row as ``the cost
largely disappears''; the table shows ES 0.108 \emph{above} S there,
which is a gain, while F1 is 0.032 below. The mechanism of the ES gain
under CS is not established in the sources.}

\subsection{The assumed half-life of $D_{bc}$}
\label{sec:invf-dbc}

\textbf{The probability limit under an assumed half-life.} Let the data
follow graded covariance binding (Equation~\eqref{eq:cg-graded}) with
half-life $t_{1/2}$, so that on path $p$ the true slope of $Y$ on $B$ is
$\mathbf s_p = -c_{bm}(\sigma_{BR}/\sigma_{bm})(0, g_1, \dots, g_8)'$,
the leading 0 for the baseline row. Let the analysis use $D_{bc,it}$
built at an assumed $t_{1/2}^a$: $x_t = 1$ on drug, $2^{-t_{sd}/t_{1/2}^a}$
off drug after exposure, 0 at baseline and before exposure, with the
biomarker block $(B_i, B_i x_t)$. By Equation~\eqref{eq:bp-plim}, if
$t_{1/2}^a = t_{1/2}$ then $\mathbf s_p = -c_{bm}(\sigma_{BR}/\sigma_{bm})
\,\mathbf x_p$ lies in the span of $Z_p = (\mathbf 1, \mathbf x_p)$ on
every path, so
\begin{equation}
\operatorname{plim}\hat\beta_{bm:D} = -c_{bm}\,\sigma_{BR}/\sigma_{bm}
  \qquad (t_{1/2}^a = t_{1/2}),
\label{eq:invf-dbc-exact}
\end{equation}
the full on-drug interaction, under every working covariance (derived).
Otherwise the limit is the second element of
Equation~\eqref{eq:bp-plim} and depends on $V$; $\beta_{bm:D}$ is then
the interaction per unit of modeled exposure, so its scale depends on
$t_{1/2}^a$, and an assumed half-life longer than the true one overshoots
because the analysis attributes exposure the off-drug visits do not
carry. Table~\ref{tab:invf-dbc} evaluates the limit with $V$ the true
outcome covariance conditional on $B$ (the target of an unstructured
fit) and under independence.

simulated mean of \code{us-KR}, 250 replicates (Source: \code{docs/45},

\noindent The limits under the true covariance reproduce every simulated
